| `FC06-C02-U014` | **Construction — kernel and injective sheaf morphisms**                                         | §1 Sheaves; pp. 60–68; L1708–1710                         | For sheaves, the presheaf kernel is already a sheaf; φ is injective when ker φ=0, equivalently φ(U) is injective for every open U.                                                                                                                                                  | `FC06-C02-U011`, `FC06-C02-U013`                                                    |
| `FC06-C02-U015` | **Construction — image and surjective sheaf morphisms**                                         | §1 Sheaves; pp. 60–68; L1712–1714                         | The image sheaf im φ is the sheafification of the presheaf image and embeds in G; φ is surjective when im φ=G.                                                                                                                                                                      | `FC06-C02-U011`, `FC06-C02-U012`                                                    |
| `FC06-C02-U016` | **Definition — exact sequence of sheaves**                                                      | §1 Sheaves; pp. 60–68; L1716–1718                         | A sequence of sheaves is exact when ker φ^i=im φ^{i−1} at every stage; injectivity and surjectivity are the corresponding short exact endpoint conditions.                                                                                                                          | `FC06-C02-U014`, `FC06-C02-U015`                                                    |
| `FC06-C02-U017` | **Construction — quotient sheaf and cokernel**                                                  | §1 Sheaves; pp. 60–68; L1720–1722                         | For F′⊆F, the quotient sheaf F/F′ is the sheafification of U↦F(U)/F′(U); coker φ is the sheafification of the presheaf cokernel.                                                                                                                                                    | `FC06-C02-U013`, `FC06-C02-U011`, `FC06-C02-U012`                                   |
| `FC06-C02-U018` | **Comparison — exactness is stalkwise**                                                         | §1 Sheaves; pp. 60–68; L1724                              | Surjectivity need not hold on sections over every open set, but a sheaf morphism is surjective iff all stalk maps are; more generally a sequence of sheaves is exact iff every stalk sequence is exact.                                                                             | `FC06-C02-U008`, `FC06-C02-U016`                                                    |
| `FC06-C02-U019` | **Definition — direct and inverse image sheaves**                                               | §1 Sheaves; pp. 60–68; L1728–1730                         | For continuous f:X→Y, (f_*F)(V)=F(f^{-1}V); f^{-1}G is the sheafification of U↦lim_{V⊇f(U)}G(V). These define functors on sheaf categories.                                                                                                                                         | `FC06-C02-U012`                                                                     |
| `FC06-C02-U020` | **Definition — restriction of a sheaf**                                                         | §1 Sheaves; pp. 60–68; L1732                              | For an inclusion i:Z↪X, the restriction F\|_Z means i^{-1}F; its stalk at P∈Z is F_P.                                                                                                                                                                                               | `FC06-C02-U019`, `FC06-C02-U008`                                                    |
| `FC06-C02-U021` | **Construction — prime spectrum and V(a)**                                                      | §2 Schemes; pp. 69–81; L1844                              | For a ring A, Spec A is the set of prime ideals; V(a) is the set of primes containing the ideal a.                                                                                                                                                                                  | —                                                                                   |
| `FC06-C02-U022` | **Lemma 2.1 — closed-set calculus on Spec**                                                     | §2 Schemes; pp. 69–81; L1846–1848                         | For ideals a,b and {a_i}, V(ab)=V(a)∪V(b), V(Σa_i)=∩V(a_i), and V(a)⊆V(b) iff √a⊇√b.                                                                                                                                                                                                | `FC06-C02-U021`                                                                     |
| `FC06-C02-U023` | **Construction — Zariski topology on Spec**                                                     | §2 Schemes; pp. 69–81; L1854                              | The subsets V(a) are the closed sets of the Zariski topology on Spec A.                                                                                                                                                                                                             | `FC06-C02-U022`                                                                     |
| `FC06-C02-U024` | **Construction — structure sheaf on Spec**                                                      | §2 Schemes; pp. 69–81; L1856–1858                         | On U⊆Spec A, O(U) consists of functions with values in A_p locally representable as a/f with f avoiding the relevant primes; these rings and restrictions form a sheaf.                                                                                                             | `FC06-C02-U023`, `FC06-C02-U004`                                                    |
| `FC06-C02-U025` | **Definition — spectrum of a ring**                                                             | §2 Schemes; pp. 69–81; L1860                              | The spectrum of A is the topological space Spec A together with the structure sheaf O constructed from local fractions.                                                                                                                                                             | `FC06-C02-U023`, `FC06-C02-U024`                                                    |
| `FC06-C02-U026` | **Construction — basic open D(f)**                                                              | §2 Schemes; pp. 69–81; L1862                              | D(f)=Spec A\V(f); the D(f) form a basis for the topology on Spec A.                                                                                                                                                                                                                 | `FC06-C02-U023`                                                                     |
| `FC06-C02-U027` | **Proposition 2.2 — local structure of affine spectra**                                         | §2 Schemes; pp. 69–81; L1864–1868                         | For X=Spec A: O_{X,p}≅A_p; O_X(D(f))≅A_f; in particular Γ(X,O_X)≅A.                                                                                                                                                                                                                 | `FC06-C02-U025`, `FC06-C02-U008`, `FC06-C02-U026`                                   |
| `FC06-C02-U028` | **Definition — ringed and locally ringed spaces**                                               | §2 Schemes; pp. 69–81; L1906–1916                         | A ringed space is (X,O_X); a morphism is (f,f^#) with f continuous and f^#:O_Y→f_*O_X. It is locally ringed when every stalk is local, and a morphism is locally ringed when all induced stalk maps are local homomorphisms.                                                        | `FC06-C02-U019`, `FC06-C02-U008`                                                    |
| `FC06-C02-U029` | **Proposition 2.3 — Spec is contravariantly fully faithful on rings**                           | §2 Schemes; pp. 69–81; L1918–1926                         | Spec A is locally ringed; a ring homomorphism A→B induces Spec B→Spec A, and every morphism of locally ringed spaces Spec B→Spec A arises uniquely this way.                                                                                                                        | `FC06-C02-U025`, `FC06-C02-U027`, `FC06-C02-U028`                                   |
| `FC06-C02-U030` | **Comparison — locality is essential for affine morphisms**                                     | §2 Schemes; pp. 69–81; L1936                              | Without requiring stalk maps to be local homomorphisms, morphisms of ringed spaces between affine spectra need not come from ring homomorphisms.                                                                                                                                    | `FC06-C02-U029`, `FC06-C02-U028`                                                    |
| `FC06-C02-U031` | **Definition — affine scheme, scheme, morphism**                                                | §2 Schemes; pp. 69–81; L1940                              | An affine scheme is a locally ringed space isomorphic to Spec A; a scheme is locally affine. The structure sheaf is O_X, and morphisms/isomorphisms are those of locally ringed spaces.                                                                                             | `FC06-C02-U025`, `FC06-C02-U028`                                                    |
| `FC06-C02-U032` | **Example 2.3.1 — spectrum of a field**                                                         | §2 Schemes; pp. 69–81; L1942                              | Spec k has one point and structure sheaf k.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                         | `FC06-C02-U031`                                                                     |
| `FC06-C02-U033` | **Example 2.3.2 — spectrum of a DVR**                                                           | §2 Schemes; pp. 69–81; L1944                              | Spec R for a DVR has a closed point with local ring R and an open dense generic point with local ring Frac(R); the local-morphism condition distinguishes the canonical generic-point map.                                                                                          | `FC06-C02-U031`, `FC06-C02-U027`                                                    |
| `FC06-C02-U034` | **Example 2.3.3 — affine line as a scheme**                                                     | §2 Schemes; pp. 69–81; L1946                              | A^1_k=Spec k[x] has a generic point (0) and closed points given by maximal ideals; over algebraically closed k the closed points correspond to elements of k.                                                                                                                       | `FC06-C02-U031`, `FC06-C02-U021`                                                    |
| `FC06-C02-U035` | **Example 2.3.4 — affine plane and generic curve points**                                       | §2 Schemes; pp. 69–81; L1948                              | A^2_k=Spec k[x,y] has the classical affine plane as its closed points, a generic point for the whole plane, and a generic point for each irreducible curve.                                                                                                                         | `FC06-C02-U034`, `FC06-C02-U021`                                                    |
| `FC06-C02-U036` | **Example 2.3.5 — gluing schemes**                                                              | §2 Schemes; pp. 69–81; L1953–1961                         | Schemes X_1,X_2 with isomorphic open subschemes U_1≅U_2 glue to a scheme obtained by gluing the spaces and compatible structure sheaves.                                                                                                                                            | `FC06-C02-U031`, `FC06-C02-U020`                                                    |
| `FC06-C02-U037` | **Example 2.3.6 — affine line with doubled origin**                                             | §2 Schemes; pp. 69–81; L1963–1969                         | Gluing two copies of A^1 along A^1{0} produces a nonaffine scheme with doubled origin; it is later the basic nonseparated example.                                                                                                                                                  | `FC06-C02-U036`, `FC06-C02-U034`                                                    |
| `FC06-C02-U038` | **Construction — Proj S and V(a)**                                                              | §2 Schemes; pp. 69–81; L1973–1975                         | For graded S with irrelevant ideal S_+, Proj S consists of homogeneous primes not containing S_+; homogeneous ideals define subsets V(a).                                                                                                                                           | `FC06-C01-U039`                                                                     |
| `FC06-C02-U039` | **Lemma 2.4 — closed-set calculus on Proj**                                                     | §2 Schemes; pp. 69–81; L1977–1979                         | For homogeneous ideals a,b and {a_i}, V(ab)=V(a)∪V(b) and V(Σa_i)=∩V(a_i).                                                                                                                                                                                                          | `FC06-C02-U038`                                                                     |
| `FC06-C02-U040` | **Construction — Zariski topology on Proj**                                                     | §2 Schemes; pp. 69–81; L1983                              | The sets V(a) for homogeneous ideals are the closed sets of the topology on Proj S.                                                                                                                                                                                                 | `FC06-C02-U039`                                                                     |
| `FC06-C02-U041` | **Construction — structure sheaf on Proj**                                                      | §2 Schemes; pp. 69–81; L1985                              | O on Proj S has stalk targets S_(p), the degree-zero parts of homogeneous localizations, and sections locally represented by same-degree quotients a/f.                                                                                                                             | `FC06-C02-U038`, `FC06-C02-U040`, `FC06-C02-U004`                                   |
| `FC06-C02-U042` | **Definition — Proj as a ringed space**                                                         | §2 Schemes; pp. 69–81; L1987                              | Proj S means the topological space Proj S equipped with the sheaf of rings constructed from degree-zero local fractions.                                                                                                                                                            | `FC06-C02-U040`, `FC06-C02-U041`                                                    |
| `FC06-C02-U043` | **Proposition 2.5 — standard affine cover of Proj**                                             | §2 Schemes; pp. 69–81; L1989–2001                         | For p∈Proj S, O_p≅S_(p). The opens D_+(f) for homogeneous f∈S_+ cover Proj S and are isomorphic to Spec S_(f); hence Proj S is a scheme.                                                                                                                                            | `FC06-C02-U042`, `FC06-C02-U031`, `FC06-C02-U027`                                   |
| `FC06-C02-U044` | **Example 2.5.1 — projective space over a ring**                                                | §2 Schemes; pp. 69–81; L2009                              | P^n_A is defined as Proj A[x_0,…,x_n]; over algebraically closed k its closed-point subspace is classical projective n-space.                                                                                                                                                       | `FC06-C02-U043`                                                                     |
| `FC06-C02-U045` | **Definition — schemes over a base**                                                            | §2 Schemes; pp. 69–81; L2015                              | A scheme over S is a scheme X with a morphism X→S; an S-morphism commutes with structure maps. Sch(S) denotes their category.                                                                                                                                                       | `FC06-C02-U031`                                                                     |
| `FC06-C02-U046` | **Proposition 2.6 — varieties embed fully faithfully in schemes**                               | §2 Schemes; pp. 69–81; L2017–2037                         | For algebraically closed k there is a fully faithful functor t:Var(k)→Sch(k); the original variety is the closed-point subspace of t(V), and its regular-function sheaf is the restriction of O_{t(V)}.                                                                             | `FC06-C02-U045`, `FC06-C01-U053`, `FC06-C01-U068`                                   |
| `FC06-C02-U047` | **Definition — connected and irreducible schemes**                                              | §3 First Properties of Schemes; pp. 82–94; L2128          | A scheme is connected or irreducible exactly when its underlying topological space is.                                                                                                                                                                                              | `FC06-C02-U031`                                                                     |
| `FC06-C02-U048` | **Definition — reduced scheme**                                                                 | §3 First Properties of Schemes; pp. 82–94; L2130          | X is reduced when O_X(U) has no nonzero nilpotents for every open U; equivalently all local rings O_{X,P} are reduced.                                                                                                                                                              | `FC06-C02-U031`, `FC06-C02-U008`                                                    |
| `FC06-C02-U049` | **Definition — integral scheme**                                                                | §3 First Properties of Schemes; pp. 82–94; L2132          | X is integral when O_X(U) is an integral domain for every open U.                                                                                                                                                                                                                   | `FC06-C02-U031`                                                                     |
| `FC06-C02-U050` | **Example 3.0.1 — affine irreducible/reduced/integral criteria**                                | §3 First Properties of Schemes; pp. 82–94; L2134          | For X=Spec A: irreducible iff nil(A) is prime; reduced iff nil(A)=0; integral iff A is a domain.                                                                                                                                                                                    | `FC06-C02-U021`, `FC06-C02-U048`, `FC06-C02-U049`                                   |
| `FC06-C02-U051` | **Proposition 3.1 — integral iff reduced and irreducible**                                      | §3 First Properties of Schemes; pp. 82–94; L2136          | A scheme is integral iff it is both reduced and irreducible.                                                                                                                                                                                                                        | `FC06-C02-U047`, `FC06-C02-U048`, `FC06-C02-U049`                                   |
| `FC06-C02-U052` | **Definition — locally noetherian and noetherian schemes**                                      | §3 First Properties of Schemes; pp. 82–94; L2142          | X is locally noetherian if it has an affine cover by spectra of noetherian rings; it is noetherian if additionally quasi-compact, equivalently it has a finite such affine cover.                                                                                                   | `FC06-C02-U031`                                                                     |
| `FC06-C02-U053` | **Comparison — scheme noetherianity is stronger than topological noetherianity**                | §3 First Properties of Schemes; pp. 82–94; L2144          | A noetherian scheme has a noetherian underlying space, but the converse need not hold.                                                                                                                                                                                              | `FC06-C02-U052`, `FC06-C01-U023`                                                    |
| `FC06-C02-U054` | **Proposition 3.2 — noetherianity is affine-local**                                             | §3 First Properties of Schemes; pp. 82–94; L2148          | X is locally noetherian iff every open affine Spec A⊆X has A noetherian; in particular Spec A is noetherian iff A is noetherian.                                                                                                                                                    | `FC06-C02-U052`, `FC06-C02-U026`                                                    |
| `FC06-C02-U055` | **Definition — locally of finite type and finite type morphisms**                               | §3 First Properties of Schemes; pp. 82–94; L2184          | f:X→Y is locally of finite type if over an affine cover Spec B_i of Y, f^{-1}(Spec B_i) has affine covers Spec A_ij with A_ij finitely generated over B_i; finite type additionally requires finite such covers.                                                                    | `FC06-C02-U031`                                                                     |
| `FC06-C02-U056` | **Definition — finite morphism**                                                                | §3 First Properties of Schemes; pp. 82–94; L2186          | f:X→Y is finite if over an affine cover Spec B_i of Y, each inverse image is affine Spec A_i with A_i finite as a B_i-module.                                                                                                                                                       | `FC06-C02-U031`                                                                     |
| `FC06-C02-U057` | **Example 3.2.1 — associated variety schemes**                                                  | §3 First Properties of Schemes; pp. 82–94; L2190          | For a variety V over algebraically closed k, t(V) is integral, noetherian, and of finite type over k.                                                                                                                                                                               | `FC06-C02-U046`, `FC06-C02-U049`, `FC06-C02-U052`, `FC06-C02-U055`                  |
| `FC06-C02-U058` | **Example 3.2.2 — local spectra need not be finite type**                                       | §3 First Properties of Schemes; pp. 82–94; L2192          | For P on a variety V, Spec O_{V,P} is integral and noetherian but generally not of finite type over k.                                                                                                                                                                              | `FC06-C02-U027`, `FC06-C02-U049`, `FC06-C02-U052`, `FC06-C02-U055`                  |
| `FC06-C02-U059` | **Definition — open subscheme and open immersion**                                              | §3 First Properties of Schemes; pp. 82–94; L2196          | An open subscheme has an open underlying subset with restricted structure sheaf; an open immersion identifies its source with an open subscheme.                                                                                                                                    | `FC06-C02-U031`, `FC06-C02-U020`                                                    |
| `FC06-C02-U060` | **Definition — closed immersion and closed subscheme**                                          | §3 First Properties of Schemes; pp. 82–94; L2200          | A closed immersion Y→X is a homeomorphism onto a closed subset with O_X→i_*O_Y surjective; closed subschemes are closed immersions modulo isomorphism over X.                                                                                                                       | `FC06-C02-U031`, `FC06-C02-U019`                                                    |
| `FC06-C02-U061` | **Example 3.2.3 — ideals define affine closed subschemes**                                      | §3 First Properties of Schemes; pp. 82–94; L2202–2204     | For a⊆A, Spec(A/a)→Spec A is a closed immersion onto V(a); every ideal therefore defines a closed subscheme structure on V(a).                                                                                                                                                      | `FC06-C02-U060`, `FC06-C02-U029`, `FC06-C02-U021`                                   |
| `FC06-C02-U062` | **Example 3.2.4 — nilpotent and embedded structures in A²**                                     | §3 First Properties of Schemes; pp. 82–94; L2206          | Ideals (xy), (x²), and (x²,xy) in k[x,y] exhibit respectively reducible, everywhere nonreduced, and embedded-point closed subscheme structures.                                                                                                                                     | `FC06-C02-U061`, `FC06-C02-U048`                                                    |
| `FC06-C02-U063` | **Example 3.2.5 — infinitesimal neighborhoods**                                                 | §3 First Properties of Schemes; pp. 82–94; L2208          | If W⊆V corresponds to prime p and Y=t(W)⊆X=t(V), the closed subscheme Y_n defined by p^n is the nth infinitesimal neighborhood; n>1 is generally nonreduced.                                                                                                                        | `FC06-C02-U061`, `FC06-C02-U046`, `FC06-C02-U048`                                   |
| `FC06-C02-U064` | **Example 3.2.6 — reduced induced closed subscheme**                                            | §3 First Properties of Schemes; pp. 82–94; L2210–2216     | Every closed subset Y⊆X has a canonical reduced induced closed subscheme structure, obtained affinely from the intersection of primes in Y and glued across affine opens.                                                                                                           | `FC06-C02-U060`, `FC06-C02-U036`, `FC06-C02-U048`                                   |
| `FC06-C02-U065` | **Definition — dimension and codimension of schemes**                                           | §3 First Properties of Schemes; pp. 82–94; L2218–2230     | dim X is the topological dimension. For irreducible closed Z⊆X, codim(Z,X) is the supremum of lengths of irreducible chains starting at Z; for closed Y take the infimum over irreducible Z⊆Y.                                                                                      | `FC06-C01-U027`                                                                     |
| `FC06-C02-U066` | **Example 3.2.7 — dimension of an affine scheme**                                               | §3 First Properties of Schemes; pp. 82–94; L2232          | For X=Spec A, dim X equals the Krull dimension of A.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                | `FC06-C02-U065`, `FC06-C02-U021`, `FC06-C01-U029`                                   |
| `FC06-C02-U067` | **Comparison — dimension formula need not hold for arbitrary schemes**                          | §3 First Properties of Schemes; pp. 82–94; L2234          | For integral finite-type schemes over a field, dim Y+codim(Y,X)=dim X for irreducible closed Y; such behavior can fail on arbitrary noetherian schemes.                                                                                                                             | `FC06-C02-U065`, `FC06-C02-U055`, `FC06-C02-U049`                                   |
| `FC06-C02-U068` | **Definition — fibre product**                                                                  | §3 First Properties of Schemes; pp. 82–94; L2236          | For X,Y over S, X×_S Y with projections is characterized by the usual universal property for pairs of S-compatible morphisms.                                                                                                                                                       | `FC06-C02-U045`                                                                     |
| `FC06-C02-U069` | **Convention — product over Spec Z**                                                            | §3 First Properties of Schemes; pp. 82–94; L2242          | For schemes with no stated base, X×Y means X×_{Spec Z}Y.                                                                                                                                                                                                                            | `FC06-C02-U068`                                                                     |
| `FC06-C02-U070` | **Theorem 3.3 — existence of fibre products**                                                   | §3 First Properties of Schemes; pp. 82–94; L2244          | Fibre products of schemes over any base S exist and are unique up to unique isomorphism; affinely Spec A×_{Spec R}Spec B≅Spec(A⊗_R B).                                                                                                                                              | `FC06-C02-U068`, `FC06-C02-U029`                                                    |
| `FC06-C02-U071` | **Definition — fibre of a morphism**                                                            | §3 First Properties of Schemes; pp. 82–94; L2264–2270     | For f:X→Y and y∈Y with residue field k(y), the fibre is X_y=X×_Y Spec k(y); its underlying space is f^{-1}(y).                                                                                                                                                                      | `FC06-C02-U070`, `FC06-C02-U032`                                                    |
| `FC06-C02-U072` | **Construction — families and deformations**                                                    | §3 First Properties of Schemes; pp. 82–94; L2272          | A family of deformations of X_0/k is a morphism f:X→Y with Y connected and a k-rational y_0 such that X_{y_0}≅X_0; other fibres are deformations.                                                                                                                                   | `FC06-C02-U071`, `FC06-C02-U047`                                                    |
| `FC06-C02-U073` | **Construction — reduction modulo p**                                                           | §3 First Properties of Schemes; pp. 82–94; L2274          | For X over Spec Z, the generic fibre X_Q is over Q and the fibre X_p over a closed prime p is over F_p; X_p is the reduction mod p.                                                                                                                                                 | `FC06-C02-U071`, `FC06-C02-U045`                                                    |
| `FC06-C02-U074` | **Construction — base extension**                                                               | §3 First Properties of Schemes; pp. 82–94; L2276          | Given S′→S and X/S, the base extension is X_{S′}=X×_S S′; base extension is transitive up to canonical isomorphism.                                                                                                                                                                 | `FC06-C02-U070`, `FC06-C02-U045`                                                    |
| `FC06-C02-U075` | **Comparison — stability under base extension**                                                 | §3 First Properties of Schemes; pp. 82–94; L2278          | Finite type is stable under arbitrary base extension; integrality of schemes need not be, as fibres/base changes may become reducible or nonreduced.                                                                                                                                | `FC06-C02-U074`, `FC06-C02-U055`, `FC06-C02-U049`                                   |
| `FC06-C02-U076` | **Example 3.3.1 — family with nonreduced special fibre**                                        | §3 First Properties of Schemes; pp. 82–94; L2280–2286     | The family ay=x² over A¹ has integral total/base schemes and irreducible reduced fibres for a≠0, but special fibre x²=0 is nonreduced.                                                                                                                                              | `FC06-C02-U071`, `FC06-C02-U072`, `FC06-C02-U048`                                   |
| `FC06-C02-U077` | **Example 3.3.2 — family with reducible special fibre**                                         | §3 First Properties of Schemes; pp. 82–94; L2291          | The family xy=t has irreducible hyperbola fibres for t≠0 and special fibre xy=0, the union of two lines.                                                                                                                                                                            | `FC06-C02-U071`, `FC06-C02-U072`, `FC06-C02-U047`                                   |
| `FC06-C02-U078` | **Definition — diagonal morphism and separated morphism**                                       | §4 Separated and Proper Morphisms; pp. 95–107; L2417      | For f:X→Y, the diagonal Δ:X→X×_Y X is the unique map whose two projections are id_X. The morphism f is separated when Δ is a closed immersion; X is separated when separated over Spec Z.                                                                                           | `FC06-C02-U070`, `FC06-C02-U060`                                                    |
| `FC06-C02-U079` | **Example 4.0.1 — doubled-origin line is not separated**                                        | §4 Separated and Proper Morphisms; pp. 95–107; L2419      | The affine line with doubled origin is not separated over k: its diagonal misses two origins lying in the closure of the diagonal image.                                                                                                                                            | `FC06-C02-U037`, `FC06-C02-U078`                                                    |
| `FC06-C02-U080` | **Example 4.0.2 — associated varieties are separated**                                          | §4 Separated and Proper Morphisms; pp. 95–107; L2421      | For every variety V over algebraically closed k, the associated scheme t(V) is separated over k.                                                                                                                                                                                    | `FC06-C02-U046`, `FC06-C02-U078`                                                    |
| `FC06-C02-U081` | **Proposition 4.1 — affine morphisms are separated**                                            | §4 Separated and Proper Morphisms; pp. 95–107; L2423      | Every morphism of affine schemes is separated.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                      | `FC06-C02-U029`, `FC06-C02-U078`                                                    |
| `FC06-C02-U082` | **Corollary 4.2 — topological diagonal criterion**                                              | §4 Separated and Proper Morphisms; pp. 95–107; L2427–2435 | A morphism f:X→Y is separated iff the image Δ(X) is closed in X×_Y X.                                                                                                                                                                                                               | `FC06-C02-U078`, `FC06-C02-U081`                                                    |
| `FC06-C02-U083` | **Theorem 4.3 — valuative criterion of separatedness**                                          | §4 Separated and Proper Morphisms; pp. 95–107; L2437–2443 | If X is noetherian, f:X→Y is separated iff for every valuation ring R with fraction field K, any two Y-morphisms Spec R→X agreeing on Spec K are equal.                                                                                                                             | `FC06-C02-U078`, `FC06-C02-U052`                                                    |
| `FC06-C02-U084` | **Lemma 4.4 — maps from a valuation spectrum**                                                  | §4 Separated and Proper Morphisms; pp. 95–107; L2445–2449 | A map Spec K→X is a point x₁ with k(x₁)↪K. A map Spec R→X is a specialization x₀ of x₁ together with such a field inclusion for which R dominates the local ring of x₀ on the reduced closure of x₁.                                                                                | `FC06-C02-U064`, `FC06-C02-U008`, `FC06-C01-U110`, `FC06-C01-U111`                  |
| `FC06-C02-U085` | **Lemma 4.5 — quasi-compact image criterion**                                                   | §4 Separated and Proper Morphisms; pp. 95–107; L2451–2461 | For a quasi-compact morphism f:X→Y, f(X) is closed iff it is stable under specialization.                                                                                                                                                                                           | `FC06-C02-U031`                                                                     |
| `FC06-C02-U086` | **Corollary 4.6 — stability properties of separated morphisms**                                 | §4 Separated and Proper Morphisms; pp. 95–107; L2463–2478 | For noetherian schemes: open/closed immersions are separated; separated morphisms are closed under composition, base change, and products; if g∘f is separated then f is; and separatedness is local on the base.                                                                   | `FC06-C02-U078`, `FC06-C02-U074`, `FC06-C02-U060`, `FC06-C02-U059`                  |
| `FC06-C02-U087` | **Definition — proper and universally closed morphisms**                                        | §4 Separated and Proper Morphisms; pp. 95–107; L2480      | f:X→Y is proper when it is separated, of finite type, and universally closed; universally closed means every base change is a closed map.                                                                                                                                           | `FC06-C02-U078`, `FC06-C02-U055`, `FC06-C02-U074`                                   |
| `FC06-C02-U088` | **Example 4.6.1 — affine line is not proper**                                                   | §4 Separated and Proper Morphisms; pp. 95–107; L2482      | A¹_k is separated and finite type but not proper: after base change, projection A²→A¹ sends the closed hyperbola xy=1 to A¹{0}, which is not closed.                                                                                                                                | `FC06-C02-U087`, `FC06-C02-U034`                                                    |
| `FC06-C02-U089` | **Theorem 4.7 — valuative criterion of properness**                                             | §4 Separated and Proper Morphisms; pp. 95–107; L2486–2510 | If f:X→Y is finite type with X noetherian, then f is proper iff every commutative diagram Spec K→X over Spec R→Y for a valuation ring R has a unique lift Spec R→X.                                                                                                                 | `FC06-C02-U087`, `FC06-C02-U083`, `FC06-C02-U052`                                   |
| `FC06-C02-U090` | **Corollary 4.8 — stability properties of proper morphisms**                                    | §4 Separated and Proper Morphisms; pp. 95–107; L2512–2522 | For noetherian schemes: closed immersions are proper; proper morphisms are closed under composition, base change, and products; if g∘f is proper and g separated then f is proper; properness is local on the base.                                                                 | `FC06-C02-U087`, `FC06-C02-U086`, `FC06-C02-U089`                                   |
| `FC06-C02-U091` | **Definition — projective and quasi-projective morphisms**                                      | §4 Separated and Proper Morphisms; pp. 95–107; L2524      | P^n_Y=P^n_Z×Y. A morphism X→Y is projective if it factors as a closed immersion into P^n_Y followed by projection; it is quasi-projective if it factors as an open immersion followed by a projective morphism.                                                                     | `FC06-C02-U044`, `FC06-C02-U074`, `FC06-C02-U060`, `FC06-C02-U059`                  |
| `FC06-C02-U092` | **Example 4.8.1 — Proj of a degree-one generated algebra is projective**                        | §4 Separated and Proper Morphisms; pp. 95–107; L2526      | If S_0=A and S is finitely generated over A by S_1, then Proj S→Spec A is projective via a closed immersion into projective space.                                                                                                                                                  | `FC06-C02-U043`, `FC06-C02-U091`                                                    |
| `FC06-C02-U093` | **Theorem 4.9 — projective implies proper**                                                     | §4 Separated and Proper Morphisms; pp. 95–107; L2528–2548 | A projective morphism of noetherian schemes is proper; a quasi-projective morphism of noetherian schemes is finite type and separated.                                                                                                                                              | `FC06-C02-U091`, `FC06-C02-U087`                                                    |
| `FC06-C02-U094` | **Proposition 4.10 — scheme-theoretic image of classical varieties**                            | §4 Separated and Proper Morphisms; pp. 95–107; L2550–2554 | Over algebraically closed k, the essential image of t:Var(k)→Sch(k) is exactly the quasi-projective integral k-schemes; projective varieties correspond to projective integral k-schemes. Thus t(V) is integral, separated, finite type.                                            | `FC06-C02-U046`, `FC06-C02-U049`, `FC06-C02-U091`, `FC06-C02-U093`                  |
| `FC06-C02-U095` | **Definition — abstract variety and complete variety**                                          | §4 Separated and Proper Morphisms; pp. 95–107; L2556      | An abstract variety over algebraically closed k is an integral separated finite-type k-scheme; it is complete when proper over k.                                                                                                                                                   | `FC06-C02-U049`, `FC06-C02-U078`, `FC06-C02-U055`, `FC06-C02-U087`                  |
| `FC06-C02-U096` | **Remark 4.10.1 — scheme-theoretic variety convention**                                         | §4 Separated and Proper Morphisms; pp. 95–107; L2558      | From here on, “variety” means abstract variety; Chapter-I varieties are identified with their associated quasi-projective schemes; curve/surface/threefold mean dimensions 1/2/3.                                                                                                   | `FC06-C02-U095`, `FC06-C02-U046`, `FC06-C02-U065`                                   |
| `FC06-C02-U097` | **Remark 4.10.2 — complete versus projective varieties**                                        | §4 Separated and Proper Morphisms; pp. 95–107; L2560–2572 | Complete curves are projective and nonsingular complete surfaces are projective, but complete nonprojective varieties exist in dimensions ≥2; every variety admits an open dense embedding into a complete variety.                                                                 | `FC06-C02-U095`, `FC06-C02-U091`                                                    |
| `FC06-C02-U098` | **Theorem 4.11A — integral closure as intersection of valuation rings**                         | §4 Separated and Proper Morphisms; pp. 95–107; L2574      | For A⊆K a field, the integral closure of A in K is the intersection of all valuation rings of K containing A.                                                                                                                                                                       | `FC06-C01-U110`                                                                     |
| `FC06-C02-U099` | **Definitions — sheaf of modules and module morphism**                                          | §5 Sheaves of Modules; pp. 108–128; L2668                 | An O_X-module is a sheaf F whose F(U) are O_X(U)-modules compatibly with restrictions; a morphism is O_X-linear on every open set.                                                                                                                                                  | `FC06-C02-U004`, `FC06-C02-U028`                                                    |
| `FC06-C02-U100` | **Construction/convention — exact and additive operations on O_X-modules**                      | §5 Sheaves of Modules; pp. 108–128; L2670                 | Kernels, cokernels, images, quotients, sums, products, direct/inverse limits remain O_X-modules; Hom_X(F,G) denotes module morphisms; exactness is exactness of underlying abelian sheaves.                                                                                         | `FC06-C02-U099`, `FC06-C02-U016`, `FC06-C02-U017`                                   |
| `FC06-C02-U101` | **Construction — internal sheaf Hom**                                                           | §5 Sheaves of Modules; pp. 108–128; L2672–2678            | The assignment U↦Hom_{O_X\|U}(F\|U,G\|U) is a sheaf O_X-module, denoted Hom_OX(F,G).                                                                                                                                                                                                | `FC06-C02-U099`, `FC06-C02-U020`                                                    |
| `FC06-C02-U102` | **Construction — tensor product of sheaves of modules**                                         | §5 Sheaves of Modules; pp. 108–128; L2680                 | F⊗*{O_X}G is the sheafification of U↦F(U)⊗*{O_X(U)}G(U).                                                                                                                                                                                                                            | `FC06-C02-U099`, `FC06-C02-U012`                                                    |
| `FC06-C02-U103` | **Definition — free, locally free, rank, invertible sheaf**                                     | §5 Sheaves of Modules; pp. 108–128; L2682                 | F is free if a direct sum of copies of O_X; locally free if so on an open cover. Rank is the local number of copies, constant on connected X; rank one means invertible.                                                                                                            | `FC06-C02-U099`, `FC06-C02-U047`                                                    |
| `FC06-C02-U104` | **Definition — sheaf of ideals**                                                                | §5 Sheaves of Modules; pp. 108–128; L2684                 | A sheaf of ideals I on X is an O_X-submodule of O_X, equivalently I(U) is an ideal of O_X(U) for every open U.                                                                                                                                                                      | `FC06-C02-U099`, `FC06-C02-U013`                                                    |
| `FC06-C02-U105` | **Construction — direct image of modules**                                                      | §5 Sheaves of Modules; pp. 108–128; L2686                 | For f:(X,O_X)→(Y,O_Y) and O_X-module F, f_*F becomes an O_Y-module via O_Y→f_*O_X.                                                                                                                                                                                                  | `FC06-C02-U099`, `FC06-C02-U028`, `FC06-C02-U019`                                   |
| `FC06-C02-U106` | **Construction — inverse image/pullback of modules**                                            | §5 Sheaves of Modules; pp. 108–128; L2688–2694            | For an O_Y-module G, define f^*G=f^{-1}G⊗_{f^{-1}O_Y}O_X; it is an O_X-module.                                                                                                                                                                                                      | `FC06-C02-U105`, `FC06-C02-U102`                                                    |
| `FC06-C02-U107` | **Comparison — pullback/direct-image adjunction**                                               | §5 Sheaves of Modules; pp. 108–128; L2696–2700            | For O_X-module F and O_Y-module G there is a natural isomorphism Hom_X(f^*G,F)≅Hom_Y(G,f_*F).                                                                                                                                                                                       | `FC06-C02-U105`, `FC06-C02-U106`                                                    |
| `FC06-C02-U108` | **Definition — sheaf associated to a module on Spec**                                           | §5 Sheaves of Modules; pp. 108–128; L2704                 | For A-module M, the sheaf M~ on Spec A has stalk values M_p and sections locally represented by fractions m/f.                                                                                                                                                                      | `FC06-C02-U025`, `FC06-C02-U099`                                                    |
| `FC06-C02-U109` | **Proposition 5.1 — affine associated-sheaf properties**                                        | §5 Sheaves of Modules; pp. 108–128; L2706–2712            | For X=Spec A: M~ is an O_X-module, (M~)_p≅M_p, Γ(D(f),M~)≅M_f, and Γ(X,M~)≅M.                                                                                                                                                                                                       | `FC06-C02-U108`, `FC06-C02-U026`, `FC06-C02-U008`                                   |
| `FC06-C02-U110` | **Proposition 5.2 — functoriality of associated sheaves**                                       | §5 Sheaves of Modules; pp. 108–128; L2714–2722            | M↦M~ is exact and fully faithful; it commutes with tensor products and direct sums. For Spec B→Spec A, direct image corresponds to restriction of scalars and pullback to M⊗_A B.                                                                                                   | `FC06-C02-U109`, `FC06-C02-U105`, `FC06-C02-U106`, `FC06-C02-U102`                  |
| `FC06-C02-U111` | **Definition — quasi-coherent and coherent sheaves**                                            | §5 Sheaves of Modules; pp. 108–128; L2724                 | An O_X-module F is quasi-coherent if locally on affine U_i=Spec A_i it is M_i~; it is coherent if the M_i can be finitely generated (normally used on noetherian schemes).                                                                                                          | `FC06-C02-U108`, `FC06-C02-U052`                                                    |
| `FC06-C02-U112` | **Example 5.2.1 — structure sheaf is quasi-coherent/coherent**                                  | §5 Sheaves of Modules; pp. 108–128; L2728                 | O_X is quasi-coherent, in fact coherent, on every scheme.                                                                                                                                                                                                                           | `FC06-C02-U111`                                                                     |
| `FC06-C02-U113` | **Example 5.2.2 — structure sheaf of an affine closed subscheme**                               | §5 Sheaves of Modules; pp. 108–128; L2730                 | If X=Spec A and Y=Spec(A/a) is closed with inclusion i, then i_*O_Y≅(A/a)~, hence is quasi-coherent and coherent.                                                                                                                                                                   | `FC06-C02-U061`, `FC06-C02-U105`, `FC06-C02-U111`                                   |
| `FC06-C02-U114` | **Example 5.2.3 — extension by zero need not be quasi-coherent**                                | §5 Sheaves of Modules; pp. 108–128; L2732                 | For an open immersion j:U→X, extension by zero j_!O_U is an O_X-module but need not be quasi-coherent.                                                                                                                                                                              | `FC06-C02-U111`, `FC06-C02-U059`                                                    |
| `FC06-C02-U115` | **Example 5.2.4 — topological restriction need not be an O_Y-module**                           | §5 Sheaves of Modules; pp. 108–128; L2734                 | For a closed subscheme Y⊆X, the sheaf O_X\|_Y is generally not quasi-coherent on Y and may not even carry an O_Y-module structure.                                                                                                                                                  | `FC06-C02-U060`, `FC06-C02-U111`                                                    |
| `FC06-C02-U116` | **Example 5.2.5 — constant function-field sheaf**                                               | §5 Sheaves of Modules; pp. 108–128; L2736                 | On an integral noetherian scheme, the constant sheaf K of the function field is quasi-coherent but not coherent unless X is a point.                                                                                                                                                | `FC06-C02-U049`, `FC06-C02-U052`, `FC06-C02-U111`                                   |
| `FC06-C02-U117` | **Lemma 5.3 — sections on a principal affine open**                                             | §5 Sheaves of Modules; pp. 108–128; L2738–2744            | For quasi-coherent F on X=Spec A and D(f): a global section restricting to zero is killed by some f^n; every section on D(f), after multiplication by some f^n, extends globally.                                                                                                   | `FC06-C02-U111`, `FC06-C02-U026`                                                    |
| `FC06-C02-U118` | **Proposition 5.4 — affine-local criterion for quasi-coherence/coherence**                      | §5 Sheaves of Modules; pp. 108–128; L2746–2750            | F is quasi-coherent iff on every affine open Spec A it is M~; if X is noetherian, coherence is equivalently the same with M finitely generated.                                                                                                                                     | `FC06-C02-U111`, `FC06-C02-U108`, `FC06-C02-U052`                                   |
| `FC06-C02-U119` | **Corollary 5.5 — modules versus quasi-coherent sheaves on affine schemes**                     | §5 Sheaves of Modules; pp. 108–128; L2752                 | For X=Spec A, M↦M~ and Γ(X,−) are inverse equivalences A-Mod≃QCoh(X); for noetherian A they restrict to finite A-modules≃coherent sheaves.                                                                                                                                          | `FC06-C02-U109`, `FC06-C02-U118`                                                    |
| `FC06-C02-U120` | **Proposition 5.6 — exactness of global sections on affine schemes**                            | §5 Sheaves of Modules; pp. 108–128; L2756–2766            | If X is affine and 0→F′→F→F″→0 is exact with F′ quasi-coherent, then 0→Γ(X,F′)→Γ(X,F)→Γ(X,F″)→0 is exact.                                                                                                                                                                           | `FC06-C02-U016`, `FC06-C02-U111`, `FC06-C02-U117`                                   |
| `FC06-C02-U121` | **Remark 5.6.1 — affine exactness and H¹**                                                      | §5 Sheaves of Modules; pp. 108–128; L2768                 | The preceding exactness is later recovered from H¹(X,F′)=0 for quasi-coherent F′ on affine X.                                                                                                                                                                                       | `FC06-C02-U120`                                                                     |
| `FC06-C02-U122` | **Proposition 5.7 — closure properties of quasi-coherent/coherent sheaves**                     | §5 Sheaves of Modules; pp. 108–128; L2770–2780            | Kernels, cokernels, images, and extensions of quasi-coherent sheaves are quasi-coherent; on noetherian schemes the same holds for coherent sheaves.                                                                                                                                 | `FC06-C02-U100`, `FC06-C02-U111`, `FC06-C02-U118`                                   |
| `FC06-C02-U123` | **Proposition 5.8 — quasi-coherence under pullback and pushforward**                            | §5 Sheaves of Modules; pp. 108–128; L2782–2794            | Pullback preserves quasi-coherence and, for noetherian X,Y, coherence. Pushforward preserves quasi-coherence when X is noetherian or f is quasi-compact separated.                                                                                                                  | `FC06-C02-U105`, `FC06-C02-U106`, `FC06-C02-U111`, `FC06-C02-U078`                  |
| `FC06-C02-U124` | **Comparison 5.8.1 — pushforward need not preserve coherence**                                  | §5 Sheaves of Modules; pp. 108–128; L2796                 | Pushforward of a coherent sheaf need not be coherent in general; it is coherent for finite or projective morphisms, and more generally for proper morphisms.                                                                                                                        | `FC06-C02-U123`, `FC06-C02-U056`, `FC06-C02-U091`, `FC06-C02-U087`                  |
| `FC06-C02-U125` | **Definition — ideal sheaf of a closed subscheme**                                              | §5 Sheaves of Modules; pp. 108–128; L2800                 | For closed immersion i:Y→X, I_Y is ker(O_X→i_*O_Y).                                                                                                                                                                                                                                 | `FC06-C02-U060`, `FC06-C02-U105`, `FC06-C02-U104`                                   |
| `FC06-C02-U126` | **Proposition 5.9 — quasi-coherent ideal sheaves classify closed subschemes**                   | §5 Sheaves of Modules; pp. 108–128; L2802–2806            | I_Y is quasi-coherent (coherent if X noetherian), and every quasi-coherent ideal sheaf on X determines a unique closed subscheme.                                                                                                                                                   | `FC06-C02-U125`, `FC06-C02-U111`                                                    |
| `FC06-C02-U127` | **Corollary 5.10 — affine ideals classify closed subschemes**                                   | §5 Sheaves of Modules; pp. 108–128; L2808–2812            | For X=Spec A, ideals a⊆A are in bijection with closed subschemes Spec(A/a)↪X; hence every closed subscheme of an affine scheme is affine.                                                                                                                                           | `FC06-C02-U061`, `FC06-C02-U126`, `FC06-C02-U119`                                   |
| `FC06-C02-U128` | **Definition — sheaf associated to a graded module on Proj**                                    | §5 Sheaves of Modules; pp. 108–128; L2814                 | For graded S-module M, M~ on Proj S has stalk M_(p), the degree-zero part of homogeneous localization, and sections locally represented by same-degree fractions.                                                                                                                   | `FC06-C02-U042`, `FC06-C02-U111`                                                    |
| `FC06-C02-U129` | **Proposition 5.11 — associated graded sheaf properties**                                       | §5 Sheaves of Modules; pp. 108–128; L2816–2820            | For X=Proj S: (M~)*p=M*(p); on D_+(f), M~ is associated to M_(f); M~ is quasi-coherent, and coherent if S is noetherian and M finite.                                                                                                                                               | `FC06-C02-U128`, `FC06-C02-U043`, `FC06-C02-U111`                                   |

| `FC06-C02-U163` | **Proposition 6.5 — class group under deleting a closed subset**                                | §6 Divisors; pp. 129–148; L3161                           | If X satisfies (*) and U=X\Z for proper closed Z, Cl X→Cl U is surjective; it is an isomorphism if codim(Z,X)≥2, and for irreducible codimension-one Z there is an exact sequence Z→Cl X→Cl U→0.                                                                                    | `FC06-C02-U152`, `FC06-C02-U157`, `FC06-C02-U065`                                   |

| `FC06-C02-U190` | **Remark 6.12.1 — Picard group as H¹ of units**                                                 | §6 Divisors; pp. 129–148; L3368                           | Later Pic X is identified with H^1(X,O_X^*).                                                                                                                                                                                                                                        | `FC06-C02-U189`                                                                     |
| `FC06-C02-U191` | **Definition — invertible sheaf associated to a Cartier divisor**                               | §6 Divisors; pp. 129–148; L3370                           | If D is represented by local functions f_i, L(D) is the O_X-subsheaf of K locally generated by f_i^{-1}.                                                                                                                                                                            | `FC06-C02-U182`, `FC06-C02-U181`, `FC06-C02-U099`                                   |
| `FC06-C02-U192` | **Proposition 6.13 — Cartier divisors and invertible subsheaves**                               | §6 Divisors; pp. 129–148; L3372–3382                      | L(D) is invertible; D↦L(D) bijects Cartier divisors with invertible subsheaves of K; L(D_1−D_2)≅L(D_1)⊗L(D_2)^{-1}; and D_1∼D_2 iff L(D_1)≅L(D_2).                                                                                                                                  | `FC06-C02-U191`, `FC06-C02-U188`                                                    |
| `FC06-C02-U193` | **Corollary 6.14 — Cartier classes inject into Pic**                                            | §6 Divisors; pp. 129–148; L3384                           | D↦L(D) induces an injective homomorphism CaCl X→Pic X.                                                                                                                                                                                                                              | `FC06-C02-U192`, `FC06-C02-U189`                                                    |
| `FC06-C02-U194` | **Remark 6.14.1 — CaCl→Pic need not be surjective**                                             | §6 Divisors; pp. 129–148; L3386                           | CaCl X→Pic X can fail to be surjective in general, though it is an isomorphism for projective schemes over a field and, as shown next, for integral schemes.                                                                                                                        | `FC06-C02-U193`, `FC06-C02-U091`                                                    |
| `FC06-C02-U195` | **Proposition 6.15 — Cartier classes equal Pic on integral schemes**                            | §6 Divisors; pp. 129–148; L3388–3390                      | If X is integral, CaCl X→Pic X is an isomorphism.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                   | `FC06-C02-U193`, `FC06-C02-U049`, `FC06-C02-U181`                                   |
| `FC06-C02-U196` | **Corollary 6.16 — Weil classes equal Pic on locally factorial schemes**                        | §6 Divisors; pp. 129–148; L3392                           | If X is noetherian, integral, separated, and locally factorial, then Cl X≅Pic X.                                                                                                                                                                                                    | `FC06-C02-U183`, `FC06-C02-U195`                                                    |
| `FC06-C02-U197` | **Corollary 6.17 — Picard group of projective space**                                           | §6 Divisors; pp. 129–148; L3396–3398                      | Every invertible sheaf on P^n_k is isomorphic to O(l) for a unique class l∈Z; Pic(P^n_k) is generated by O(1).                                                                                                                                                                      | `FC06-C02-U196`, `FC06-C02-U162`, `FC06-C02-U130`                                   |
| `FC06-C02-U198` | **Definition — effective Cartier divisor and associated closed subscheme**                      | §6 Divisors; pp. 129–148; L3402                           | A Cartier divisor is effective if represented by regular local functions f_i; these generate an ideal sheaf defining its associated codimension-one closed subscheme.                                                                                                               | `FC06-C02-U182`, `FC06-C02-U104`, `FC06-C02-U060`                                   |
| `FC06-C02-U199` | **Remark 6.17.1 — effective Cartier divisors are locally principal subschemes**                 | §6 Divisors; pp. 129–148; L3404                           | Effective Cartier divisors correspond bijectively to locally principal closed subschemes; on integral separated noetherian locally factorial schemes they coincide with effective Weil divisors.                                                                                    | `FC06-C02-U198`, `FC06-C02-U183`                                                    |
| `FC06-C02-U200` | **Proposition 6.18 — ideal sheaf of an effective Cartier divisor**                              | §6 Divisors; pp. 129–148; L3406–3408                      | If D is effective Cartier with associated closed subscheme Y, then I_Y≅L(−D).                                                                                                                                                                                                       | `FC06-C02-U198`, `FC06-C02-U191`                                                    |
| `FC06-C02-U201` | **Theorem 7.1 — morphisms to projective space from generated line bundles**                     | §7 Projective Morphisms; pp. 149–171; L3540–3544          | For X over A, an A-morphism φ:X→P^n_A pulls O(1) back to an invertible sheaf generated by φ^*x_0,…,φ^*x_n. Conversely, an invertible sheaf L with n+1 generating global sections determines a unique such morphism with L≅φ^*O(1).                                                  | `FC06-C02-U139`, `FC06-C02-U142`, `FC06-C02-U106`                                   |
| `FC06-C02-U202` | **Example 7.1.1 — projective linear automorphisms**                                             | §7 Projective Morphisms; pp. 149–171; L3546–3548          | An invertible (n+1)×(n+1) matrix over k induces an automorphism of P^n_k, and scalar multiples induce the same map; hence PGL(n,k)=GL(n+1,k)/k^* acts by projective automorphisms.                                                                                                  | `FC06-C02-U044`, `FC06-C02-U201`                                                    |
| `FC06-C02-U203` | **Example 7.1.2 — sections define a morphism on their generation locus**                        | §7 Projective Morphisms; pp. 149–171; L3550–3552          | Arbitrary sections s_0,…,s_n of an invertible L define a morphism to P^n on the open locus where they generate L; for O(1) on P^{n+1} omitting one coordinate gives projection from the missing point.                                                                              | `FC06-C02-U201`, `FC06-C02-U142`                                                    |
| `FC06-C02-U204` | **Proposition 7.2 — affine-chart criterion for a projective morphism to be a closed immersion** | §7 Projective Morphisms; pp. 149–171; L3554–3561          | For φ:X→P^n_A determined by L and s_i, φ is a closed immersion iff every X_i={s_i≠0} is affine and each A[y_0,…,y_n]→Γ(X_i,O_X), y_j↦s_j/s_i, is surjective.                                                                                                                        | `FC06-C02-U201`, `FC06-C02-U060`                                                    |
| `FC06-C02-U205` | **Proposition 7.3 — point/tangent separation criterion**                                        | §7 Projective Morphisms; pp. 149–171; L3563–3570          | If X is projective over algebraically closed k and V⊆Γ(X,L) is spanned by sections defining φ:X→P^n, then φ is a closed immersion iff V separates distinct closed points and separates tangent vectors at every closed point.                                                       | `FC06-C02-U204`, `FC06-C02-U091`, `FC06-C01-U094`                                   |
| `FC06-C02-U206` | **Lemma 7.4 — local criterion for surjectivity of a finite algebra map**                        | §7 Projective Morphisms; pp. 149–171; L3572–3582          | For a local map A→B of noetherian local rings inducing an isomorphism on residue fields, surjective on m_A/m_A²→m_B/m_B², and with B finite as an A-module, the map A→B is surjective.                                                                                              | `FC06-C02-U052`                                                                     |
| `FC06-C02-U207` | **Definition — ample invertible sheaf**                                                         | §7 Projective Morphisms; pp. 149–171; L3584               | An invertible L on noetherian X is ample if for every coherent F there exists n_0 such that F⊗L^n is generated by global sections for all n≥n_0.                                                                                                                                    | `FC06-C02-U103`, `FC06-C02-U111`, `FC06-C02-U142`, `FC06-C02-U102`                  |
| `FC06-C02-U208` | **Remark 7.4.1 — ample is absolute, very ample is relative**                                    | §7 Projective Morphisms; pp. 149–171; L3586               | Ampleness depends only on X, while very ampleness is relative to a chosen morphism X→Y.                                                                                                                                                                                             | `FC06-C02-U207`, `FC06-C02-U140`                                                    |
| `FC06-C02-U209` | **Example 7.4.2 — every invertible sheaf on an affine scheme is ample**                         | §7 Projective Morphisms; pp. 149–171; L3588               | On an affine scheme every invertible sheaf is ample because every coherent sheaf is globally generated.                                                                                                                                                                             | `FC06-C02-U207`, `FC06-C02-U143`                                                    |
| `FC06-C02-U210` | **Remark 7.4.3 — ample as stable very ampleness**                                               | §7 Projective Morphisms; pp. 149–171; L3590               | Very ample implies ample on a projective scheme over a noetherian ring; conversely an ample invertible sheaf need not be very ample, but a positive tensor power is very ample.                                                                                                     | `FC06-C02-U207`, `FC06-C02-U140`, `FC06-C02-U145`                                   |
| `FC06-C02-U211` | **Remark 7.4.4 — cohomological criterion for ampleness**                                        | §7 Projective Morphisms; pp. 149–171; L3592               | A later criterion characterizes ampleness by vanishing of suitable higher cohomology groups after twisting.                                                                                                                                                                         | `FC06-C02-U207`                                                                     |
| `FC06-C02-U212` | **Proposition 7.5 — ampleness is invariant under positive powers**                              | §7 Projective Morphisms; pp. 149–171; L3594–3598          | For an invertible L on noetherian X, the following are equivalent: L is ample; every L^m with m>0 is ample; some L^m with m>0 is ample.                                                                                                                                             | `FC06-C02-U207`                                                                     |
| `FC06-C02-U213` | **Theorem 7.6 — ample iff some power is very ample**                                            | §7 Projective Morphisms; pp. 149–171; L3600–3616          | If X is finite type over a noetherian ring A, L is ample iff L^m is very ample over Spec A for some m>0.                                                                                                                                                                            | `FC06-C02-U207`, `FC06-C02-U140`, `FC06-C02-U055`                                   |
| `FC06-C02-U214` | **Example 7.6.1 — ample twists on projective space**                                            | §7 Projective Morphisms; pp. 149–171; L3618               | On P^n_k, O(l) is ample iff it is very ample iff l>0.                                                                                                                                                                                                                               | `FC06-C02-U213`, `FC06-C02-U197`, `FC06-C02-U130`                                   |
| `FC06-C02-U215` | **Example 7.6.2 — ample line bundles on a smooth quadric**                                      | §7 Projective Morphisms; pp. 149–171; L3620–3626          | On Q≅P^1×P^1, an invertible sheaf of type (a,b) is ample exactly when a>0 and b>0; such positive types are very ample via product Veronese and Segre embeddings.                                                                                                                    | `FC06-C02-U167`, `FC06-C02-U196`, `FC06-C02-U213`                                   |
| `FC06-C02-U216` | **Example 7.6.3 — ample need not be very ample**                                                | §7 Projective Morphisms; pp. 149–171; L3628               | On the nonsingular cubic curve with inflection P_0, L(P_0) is ample because L(3P_0)≅O_X(1) is very ample, but L(P_0) itself is not globally generated and hence not very ample.                                                                                                     | `FC06-C02-U179`, `FC06-C02-U213`, `FC06-C02-U191`, `FC06-C02-U142`                  |
| `FC06-C02-U217` | **Example 7.6.4 — curve ampleness by degree**                                                   | §7 Projective Morphisms; pp. 149–171; L3630               | A later consequence of Riemann–Roch is that on a complete nonsingular curve L(D) is ample iff deg D>0.                                                                                                                                                                              | `FC06-C02-U171`, `FC06-C02-U191`, `FC06-C02-U174`, `FC06-C02-U207`                  |
| `FC06-C02-U218` | **Proposition 7.7 — sections and effective divisors in a linear class**                         | §7 Projective Morphisms; pp. 149–171; L3640–3650          | For nonsingular projective X and L≅L(D_0): every nonzero section s has an effective zero divisor (s)_0∼D_0; every effective divisor linearly equivalent to D_0 arises this way; two sections give the same zero divisor iff they differ by a scalar in k^*.                         | `FC06-C02-U191`, `FC06-C02-U198`, `FC06-C02-U091`                                   |
| `FC06-C02-U219` | **Definition — complete linear system**                                                         | §7 Projective Morphisms; pp. 149–171; L3652–3654          | The complete linear system \|D_0\| is the set of effective divisors linearly equivalent to D_0; via Proposition 7.7 it is the projective space of nonzero sections of L(D_0) modulo k^*.                                                                                            | `FC06-C02-U218`                                                                     |
