| `FC06-C02-U220` | **Definition — linear system and its dimension**                                                | §7 Projective Morphisms; pp. 149–171; L3656               | A linear system is a projective linear subspace of a complete linear system, corresponding to a vector subspace V⊆Γ(X,L); its dimension is dim_k V−1.                                                                                                                               | `FC06-C02-U219`                                                                     |
| `FC06-C02-U221` | **Definition — base point of a linear system**                                                  | §7 Projective Morphisms; pp. 149–171; L3658               | P∈X is a base point of a linear system D when P lies in the support of every divisor in D.                                                                                                                                                                                          | `FC06-C02-U220`                                                                     |
| `FC06-C02-U222` | **Lemma 7.8 — base points and global generation**                                               | §7 Projective Morphisms; pp. 149–171; L3660–3662          | For D corresponding to V⊆Γ(X,L), P is a base point iff every s∈V has s_P∈m_PL_P; thus D is base-point-free iff V generates L.                                                                                                                                                       | `FC06-C02-U221`, `FC06-C02-U142`                                                    |
| `FC06-C02-U223` | **Remark 7.8.1 — base-point-free linear systems define projective maps**                        | §7 Projective Morphisms; pp. 149–171; L3664               | A base-point-free linear system together with a basis of its section space determines a morphism X→P^n; changing basis changes it only by a projective automorphism.                                                                                                                | `FC06-C02-U222`, `FC06-C02-U201`, `FC06-C02-U202`                                   |
| `FC06-C02-U224` | **Remark 7.8.2 — embedding criterion for linear systems**                                       | §7 Projective Morphisms; pp. 149–171; L3666–3671          | The morphism determined by a base-point-free linear system is a closed immersion iff the system separates points and tangent vectors.                                                                                                                                               | `FC06-C02-U223`, `FC06-C02-U205`                                                    |
| `FC06-C02-U225` | **Definition — trace of a linear system on a subvariety**                                       | §7 Projective Morphisms; pp. 149–171; L3673–3677          | For closed immersion i:Y↪X of nonsingular projective varieties and a system determined by (L,V), its trace on Y is determined by (i^*L, image(V→Γ(Y,i^*L))); geometrically it is intersection with Y for divisors not containing Y.                                                 | `FC06-C02-U220`, `FC06-C02-U106`, `FC06-C02-U060`                                   |
| `FC06-C02-U226` | **Example 7.8.3 — complete degree-d system on projective space**                                | §7 Projective Morphisms; pp. 149–171; L3679               | On P^n, all effective divisors of degree d>0 form the complete system \|O(d)\| of dimension binom(n+d,n)−1.                                                                                                                                                                         | `FC06-C02-U219`, `FC06-C02-U197`                                                    |
| `FC06-C02-U227` | **Example 7.8.4 — projective normality via complete hypersurface systems**                      | §7 Projective Morphisms; pp. 149–171; L3681               | A nonsingular projective X⊂P^n is projectively normal iff for every d>0 the linear system on X cut out by degree-d hypersurfaces is complete.                                                                                                                                       | `FC06-C02-U225`, `FC06-C02-U226`                                                    |
| `FC06-C02-U228` | **Example 7.8.5 — uniqueness of the twisted cubic up to projective automorphism**               | §7 Projective Morphisms; pp. 149–171; L3683–3685          | Every nonsingular degree-three nonplanar curve in P^3 abstractly isomorphic to P^1 is obtained from the standard twisted cubic by an automorphism of P^3, via the complete degree-three system on P^1.                                                                              | `FC06-C02-U223`, `FC06-C02-U202`, `FC06-C02-U226`                                   |
| `FC06-C02-U229` | **Example 7.8.6 — rational quartics need not be projectively equivalent**                       | §7 Projective Morphisms; pp. 149–171; L3687               | Degree-four rational curves in P^3 arise from four-dimensional subspaces of Γ(P^1,O(4)); different subspaces can yield smooth nonprojectively-equivalent quartics, with smooth embedding checked by point/tangent separation.                                                       | `FC06-C02-U223`, `FC06-C02-U205`, `FC06-C02-U214`                                   |
| `FC06-C02-U230` | **Convention — relative Proj hypothesis (+)**                                                   | §7 Projective Morphisms; pp. 149–171; L3693–3695          | For relative Proj, X is noetherian and S=⊕_{d≥0}S_d is a quasi-coherent graded O_X-algebra with S_0=O_X, S_1 coherent, and S locally generated by S_1.                                                                                                                              | `FC06-C02-U052`, `FC06-C02-U111`                                                    |
| `FC06-C02-U231` | **Construction — relative Proj and O(1)**                                                       | §7 Projective Morphisms; pp. 149–171; L3697–3699          | For (X,S) satisfying (+), glue Proj Γ(U,S) over affine opens U⊆X to obtain Proj S with projection π:Proj S→X; the local twisting sheaves glue to an invertible O_{Proj S}(1).                                                                                                       | `FC06-C02-U230`, `FC06-C02-U042`, `FC06-C02-U036`, `FC06-C02-U130`                  |
| `FC06-C02-U232` | **Example 7.8.7 — relative projective space as Proj**                                           | §7 Projective Morphisms; pp. 149–171; L3701               | If S=O_X[T_0,…,T_n], then Proj S=P^n_X with its usual O(1).                                                                                                                                                                                                                         | `FC06-C02-U231`, `FC06-C02-U091`                                                    |
| `FC06-C02-U233` | **Comparison 7.8.8 — relative O(1) need not be very ample**                                     | §7 Projective Morphisms; pp. 149–171; L3703               | For a general relative Proj, O(1) need not be very ample relative to X.                                                                                                                                                                                                             | `FC06-C02-U231`, `FC06-C02-U140`                                                    |
| `FC06-C02-U234` | **Lemma 7.9 — twisting the graded algebra by a line bundle**                                    | §7 Projective Morphisms; pp. 149–171; L3705–3711          | For invertible L on X and S′*d=S_d⊗L^d, Proj S′≅Proj S over X and O*{Proj S′}(1)≅φ^*O_{Proj S}(1)⊗π′^*L.                                                                                                                                                                            | `FC06-C02-U231`, `FC06-C02-U103`, `FC06-C02-U106`                                   |
| `FC06-C02-U235` | **Proposition 7.10 — proper/projective relative Proj**                                          | §7 Projective Morphisms; pp. 149–171; L3713–3723          | For (X,S) satisfying (+), π:Proj S→X is proper. If X admits an ample L, then π is projective, and O(1)⊗π^*L^n is very ample relative to X for n≫0.                                                                                                                                  | `FC06-C02-U231`, `FC06-C02-U087`, `FC06-C02-U207`, `FC06-C02-U140`, `FC06-C02-U234` |
| `FC06-C02-U236` | **Definition — projective space bundle P(E)**                                                   | §7 Projective Morphisms; pp. 149–171; L3725–3727          | For noetherian X and locally free coherent E, define P(E)=Proj Sym(E); locally where E is free of rank n+1 it is P^n over the base, with projection π and O(1).                                                                                                                     | `FC06-C02-U231`, `FC06-C02-U103`                                                    |
| `FC06-C02-U237` | **Proposition 7.11 — section algebra and tautological quotient on P(E)**                        | §7 Projective Morphisms; pp. 149–171; L3729–3737          | For rank E≥2, Sym(E)≅⊕*{l∈Z}π**O(l), with negative pieces zero, degree 0 equal O_X and degree 1 equal E; there is a natural surjection π^*E→O(1).                                                                                                                                   | `FC06-C02-U236`, `FC06-C02-U105`, `FC06-C02-U106`                                   |
| `FC06-C02-U238` | **Proposition 7.12 — universal property of P(E)**                                               | §7 Projective Morphisms; pp. 149–171; L3739–3747          | For g:Y→X, morphisms Y→P(E) over X are equivalent to pairs consisting of an invertible L on Y and a surjection g^*E→L.                                                                                                                                                              | `FC06-C02-U236`, `FC06-C02-U237`                                                    |
| `FC06-C02-U239` | **Definition — blowing up a coherent ideal**                                                    | §7 Projective Morphisms; pp. 149–171; L3749               | For noetherian X and coherent ideal sheaf I, the blowup of X along the closed subscheme defined by I is X~=Proj(⊕_{d≥0}I^d).                                                                                                                                                        | `FC06-C02-U231`, `FC06-C02-U126`, `FC06-C02-U111`                                   |
| `FC06-C02-U240` | **Example 7.12.1 — relative-Proj blowup agrees with the affine-space construction**             | §7 Projective Morphisms; pp. 149–171; L3751               | For X=A^n_k and the ideal of the origin, Proj(⊕I^d) is the earlier blowup in A^n×P^{n−1}, cut out by x_i y_j=x_j y_i.                                                                                                                                                               | `FC06-C02-U239`, `FC06-C01-U083`                                                    |
| `FC06-C02-U241` | **Definition — inverse image ideal sheaf**                                                      | §7 Projective Morphisms; pp. 149–171; L3753               | For f:X→Y and ideal I⊆O_Y, the inverse image ideal f^{-1}I·O_X is the ideal generated in O_X by the image of f^{-1}I→O_X.                                                                                                                                                           | `FC06-C02-U106`, `FC06-C02-U104`                                                    |
| `FC06-C02-U242` | **Comparison 7.12.2 — inverse image ideal differs from module pullback**                        | §7 Projective Morphisms; pp. 149–171; L3755–3761          | The module pullback f^*I need not inject into O_X; the inverse image ideal f^{-1}I·O_X is the image of the natural map f^*I→O_X.                                                                                                                                                    | `FC06-C02-U241`, `FC06-C02-U106`                                                    |
| `FC06-C02-U243` | **Proposition 7.13 — exceptional ideal and complement of the center**                           | §7 Projective Morphisms; pp. 149–171; L3763–3769          | For blowup π:X~→X of coherent I, the inverse image ideal I~=π^{-1}I·O_{X~} is invertible, and π is an isomorphism over X minus the center.                                                                                                                                          | `FC06-C02-U239`, `FC06-C02-U241`                                                    |
| `FC06-C02-U244` | **Proposition 7.14 — universal property of blowing up**                                         | §7 Projective Morphisms; pp. 149–171; L3771–3781          | If f:Z→X is any morphism for which f^{-1}I·O_Z is an invertible ideal, then f factors uniquely through the blowup π:X~→X.                                                                                                                                                           | `FC06-C02-U243`, `FC06-C02-U238`                                                    |
| `FC06-C02-U245` | **Corollary 7.15 — functoriality of blowup under inverse-image ideals**                         | §7 Projective Morphisms; pp. 149–171; L3783–3791          | For f:Y→X and coherent I on X, blowing up X along I and Y along f^{-1}I·O_Y yields a unique lifted map Y~→X~; if f is a closed immersion, so is the lift.                                                                                                                           | `FC06-C02-U244`, `FC06-C02-U241`, `FC06-C02-U060`                                   |
| `FC06-C02-U246` | **Definition — strict transform**                                                               | §7 Projective Morphisms; pp. 149–171; L3793               | If Y↪X is closed, its blowup along the inverse image of the center is the strict transform Y~⊆X~.                                                                                                                                                                                   | `FC06-C02-U245`                                                                     |
| `FC06-C02-U247` | **Example 7.15.1 — strict transform as closure off the center**                                 | §7 Projective Morphisms; pp. 149–171; L3795–3797          | For Y⊆A^n through the blown-up origin P, the strict transform is the closure of π^{-1}(Y{P}); this recovers the Chapter-I construction and proves that construction intrinsic.                                                                                                      | `FC06-C02-U246`, `FC06-C02-U240`, `FC06-C01-U088`                                   |
| `FC06-C02-U248` | **Proposition 7.16 — blowups of varieties**                                                     | §7 Projective Morphisms; pp. 149–171; L3799–3803          | For a variety X and nonzero coherent ideal I, X~ is a variety; π is birational, proper, and surjective; quasi-projectivity/projectivity of X is preserved, and π is projective.                                                                                                     | `FC06-C02-U239`, `FC06-C02-U095`, `FC06-C02-U087`, `FC06-C02-U091`                  |
| `FC06-C02-U249` | **Theorem 7.17 — projective birational morphisms are blowups**                                  | §7 Projective Morphisms; pp. 149–171; L3805–3821          | If X is a quasi-projective variety and f:Z→X is birational and projective, then f is isomorphic over X to the blowup of X along some coherent ideal sheaf.                                                                                                                          | `FC06-C02-U248`, `FC06-C02-U091`                                                    |
| `FC06-C02-U250` | **Remark 7.17.1 — blowup ideal is not unique**                                                  | §7 Projective Morphisms; pp. 149–171; L3823               | The coherent ideal realizing a given projective birational morphism as a blowup need not be unique.                                                                                                                                                                                 | `FC06-C02-U249`                                                                     |
| `FC06-C02-U251` | **Remark 7.17.2 — restricted centers in geometric applications**                                | §7 Projective Morphisms; pp. 149–171; L3826               | Although arbitrary coherent ideals give all projective birational morphisms as blowups, important applications often restrict to geometrically controlled centers, such as nonsingular centers in resolution or points on surfaces.                                                 | `FC06-C02-U249`                                                                     |
| `FC06-C02-U252` | **Example 7.17.3 — eliminating indeterminacy by blowing up the base ideal**                     | §7 Projective Morphisms; pp. 149–171; L3828–3836          | Given sections s_0,…,s_n of an invertible L on noetherian X, their generation locus carries a rational map to P^n; blowing up the coherent image ideal generated by the sections makes the pulled-back sections base-point-free and extends the map to the blowup.                  | `FC06-C02-U203`, `FC06-C02-U244`, `FC06-C02-U241`                                   |
| `FC06-C02-U253` | **Definition — A-derivation**                                                                   | §8 Differentials; pp. 172–189; L3919                      | For an A-algebra B and B-module M, an A-derivation d:B→M is additive, satisfies Leibniz d(bb′)=b d(b′)+b′ d(b), and vanishes on A.                                                                                                                                                  | —                                                                                   |
| `FC06-C02-U254` | **Definition — module of Kähler differentials**                                                 | §8 Differentials; pp. 172–189; L3921–3923                 | Ω_{B/A} with d:B→Ω_{B/A} represents A-derivations: every A-derivation B→M factors uniquely through a B-linear map Ω_{B/A}→M.                                                                                                                                                        | `FC06-C02-U253`                                                                     |
| `FC06-C02-U255` | **Proposition 8.1A — diagonal construction of Kähler differentials**                            | §8 Differentials; pp. 172–189; L3925                      | For I=ker(B⊗*A B→B), the B-module I/I² with d(b)=1⊗b−b⊗1 is a module of relative differentials Ω*{B/A}.                                                                                                                                                                             | `FC06-C02-U254`                                                                     |
| `FC06-C02-U256` | **Proposition 8.2A — base change and localization for differentials**                           | §8 Differentials; pp. 172–189; L3929                      | For B′=B⊗*A A′, Ω*{B′/A′}≅Ω_{B/A}⊗*B B′; for multiplicative S⊆B, Ω*{S^{-1}B/A}≅S^{-1}Ω_{B/A}.                                                                                                                                                                                       | `FC06-C02-U254`                                                                     |
| `FC06-C02-U257` | **Example 8.2.1 — differentials of a polynomial algebra**                                       | §8 Differentials; pp. 172–189; L3933                      | For B=A[x_1,…,x_n], Ω_{B/A} is free of rank n with basis dx_1,…,dx_n.                                                                                                                                                                                                               | `FC06-C02-U254`                                                                     |
| `FC06-C02-U258` | **Proposition 8.3A — first exact sequence of differentials**                                    | §8 Differentials; pp. 172–189; L3935–3941                 | For ring maps A→B→C there is a natural exact sequence Ω_{B/A}⊗*B C→Ω*{C/A}→Ω_{C/B}→0.                                                                                                                                                                                               | `FC06-C02-U254`                                                                     |
| `FC06-C02-U259` | **Proposition 8.4A — conormal exact sequence for rings**                                        | §8 Differentials; pp. 172–189; L3943–3951                 | For B an A-algebra, ideal I, and C=B/I, there is a natural exact sequence I/I²→Ω_{B/A}⊗*B C→Ω*{C/A}→0, with a mod I mapping to da⊗1.                                                                                                                                                | `FC06-C02-U254`, `FC06-C02-U255`                                                    |
| `FC06-C02-U260` | **Corollary 8.5 — finite generation of differentials**                                          | §8 Differentials; pp. 172–189; L3953                      | If B is finitely generated over A or a localization of a finitely generated A-algebra, then Ω_{B/A} is a finitely generated B-module.                                                                                                                                               | `FC06-C02-U257`, `FC06-C02-U259`, `FC06-C02-U256`                                   |
| `FC06-C02-U261` | **Theorem 8.6A — field differentials and separable generation**                                 | §8 Differentials; pp. 172–189; L3961                      | For finitely generated K/k, dim_K Ω_{K/k}≥trdeg_k K, with equality iff K/k is separably generated.                                                                                                                                                                                  | `FC06-C02-U254`, `FC06-C01-U079`                                                    |
| `FC06-C02-U262` | **Proposition 8.7 — cotangent space as fibre of differentials**                                 | §8 Differentials; pp. 172–189; L3965                      | If local B contains a field k identified with B/m, then the natural map m/m²→Ω_{B/k}⊗_B k is an isomorphism.                                                                                                                                                                        | `FC06-C02-U259`, `FC06-C01-U094`                                                    |
| `FC06-C02-U263` | **Theorem 8.8 — regular local rings via differentials**                                         | §8 Differentials; pp. 172–189; L3975–3979                 | If B is a localization of a finite-type algebra over a perfect field k equal to its residue field, then Ω_{B/k} is free of rank dim B iff B is regular local.                                                                                                                       | `FC06-C02-U260`, `FC06-C02-U262`, `FC06-C01-U094`                                   |
| `FC06-C02-U264` | **Lemma 8.9 — equal fibre and generic ranks imply freeness**                                    | §8 Differentials; pp. 172–189; L3981–3989                 | For a noetherian local domain A with residue field k and fraction field K, if finite M has dim_k(M⊗k)=dim_K(M⊗K)=r, then M is free of rank r.                                                                                                                                       | `FC06-C02-U052`                                                                     |
| `FC06-C02-U265` | **Definition — sheaf of relative differentials**                                                | §8 Differentials; pp. 172–189; L3995                      | For f:X→Y, take the diagonal Δ:X→X×*Y X, its locally closed image inside a suitable open W with ideal I, and define Ω*{X/Y}=Δ^*(I/I²).                                                                                                                                              | `FC06-C02-U070`, `FC06-C02-U060`, `FC06-C02-U106`                                   |
| `FC06-C02-U266` | **Remark 8.9.1 — quasi-coherence/coherence of Ω**                                               | §8 Differentials; pp. 172–189; L3997                      | Ω_{X/Y} is a quasi-coherent O_X-module; if Y is noetherian and f finite type, then it is coherent.                                                                                                                                                                                  | `FC06-C02-U265`, `FC06-C02-U111`, `FC06-C02-U055`                                   |
| `FC06-C02-U267` | **Remark 8.9.2 — affine compatibility and universal differential**                              | §8 Differentials; pp. 172–189; L3999–4001                 | On affines V=Spec B→U=Spec A, Ω_{V/U}≅(Ω_{B/A})~. The affine derivations glue to d:O_X→Ω_{X/Y}, so the sheaf definition can equivalently be made by gluing affine modules of differentials.                                                                                         | `FC06-C02-U265`, `FC06-C02-U108`, `FC06-C02-U255`, `FC06-C02-U036`                  |
| `FC06-C02-U268` | **Proposition 8.10 — base change for sheaves of differentials**                                 | §8 Differentials; pp. 172–189; L4003                      | For base change X′=X×*Y Y′ with projection g′:X′→X, Ω*{X′/Y′}≅g′*Ω_{X/Y}.                                                                                                                                                                                                           | `FC06-C02-U267`, `FC06-C02-U256`, `FC06-C02-U074`                                   |
| `FC06-C02-U269` | **Proposition 8.11 — transitivity exact sequence**                                              | §8 Differentials; pp. 172–189; L4007–4013                 | For X→Y→Z there is a natural exact sequence f^*Ω_{Y/Z}→Ω_{X/Z}→Ω_{X/Y}→0.                                                                                                                                                                                                           | `FC06-C02-U265`, `FC06-C02-U258`, `FC06-C02-U106`                                   |
| `FC06-C02-U270` | **Proposition 8.12 — conormal exact sequence**                                                  | §8 Differentials; pp. 172–189; L4015–4021                 | For Z↪X closed with ideal I and X→Y, there is a natural exact sequence I/I²→Ω_{X/Y}⊗O_Z→Ω_{Z/Y}→0.                                                                                                                                                                                  | `FC06-C02-U265`, `FC06-C02-U259`, `FC06-C02-U125`                                   |
| `FC06-C02-U271` | **Example 8.12.1 — differentials of relative affine space**                                     | §8 Differentials; pp. 172–189; L4023                      | For X=A^n_Y, Ω_{X/Y} is free of rank n generated by global dx_1,…,dx_n.                                                                                                                                                                                                             | `FC06-C02-U267`, `FC06-C02-U257`                                                    |
| `FC06-C02-U272` | **Theorem 8.13 — Euler sequence on projective space**                                           | §8 Differentials; pp. 172–189; L4027–4067                 | For X=P^n_A over Y=Spec A there is an exact sequence 0→Ω_{X/Y}→O_X(−1)^{⊕(n+1)}→O_X→0, the last map induced by the homogeneous coordinates.                                                                                                                                         | `FC06-C02-U265`, `FC06-C02-U044`, `FC06-C02-U130`                                   |
| `FC06-C02-U273` | **Definition — nonsingular abstract variety**                                                   | §8 Differentials; pp. 172–189; L4069                      | An abstract variety over algebraically closed k is nonsingular when all its local rings are regular local rings.                                                                                                                                                                    | `FC06-C02-U095`, `FC06-C01-U094`                                                    |
| `FC06-C02-U274` | **Theorem 8.14A — regularity localizes**                                                        | §8 Differentials; pp. 172–189; L4073                      | Any localization of a regular local ring at a prime ideal is again a regular local ring.                                                                                                                                                                                            | `FC06-C01-U094`                                                                     |
| `FC06-C02-U275` | **Theorem 8.15 — nonsingularity via locally free differentials**                                | §8 Differentials; pp. 172–189; L4079                      | For irreducible separated finite-type X over algebraically closed k, Ω_{X/k} is locally free of rank dim X iff X is a nonsingular variety.                                                                                                                                          | `FC06-C02-U265`, `FC06-C02-U263`, `FC06-C02-U264`, `FC06-C02-U273`                  |
| `FC06-C02-U276` | **Corollary 8.16 — nonsingular locus contains a dense open**                                    | §8 Differentials; pp. 172–189; L4083                      | Every variety over k has a dense open subset that is nonsingular.                                                                                                                                                                                                                   | `FC06-C02-U275`, `FC06-C02-U095`                                                    |
| `FC06-C02-U277` | **Theorem 8.17 — conormal criterion for a nonsingular subvariety**                              | §8 Differentials; pp. 172–189; L4087–4095                 | For irreducible closed Y⊂X with X nonsingular, Y is nonsingular iff Ω_{Y/k} is locally free and 0→I/I²→Ω_{X/k}⊗O_Y→Ω_{Y/k}→0 is exact; then I is locally generated by codim(Y,X) elements and I/I² is locally free of that rank.                                                    | `FC06-C02-U270`, `FC06-C02-U275`, `FC06-C02-U125`                                   |
| `FC06-C02-U278` | **Theorem 8.18 — Bertini hyperplane theorem**                                                   | §8 Differentials; pp. 172–189; L4115–4130                 | If X⊂P^n_k is nonsingular closed over algebraically closed k, there is a hyperplane H not containing X for which H∩X is regular everywhere; such hyperplanes form a dense open subset of the complete hyperplane system.                                                            | `FC06-C02-U273`, `FC06-C02-U219`, `FC06-C02-U205`                                   |
| `FC06-C02-U279` | **Remark 8.18.1 — Bertini with finitely many singular points**                                  | §8 Differentials; pp. 172–189; L4132                      | The Bertini conclusion remains valid when X has only finitely many singular points, by avoiding the proper closed sets of hyperplanes through them.                                                                                                                                 | `FC06-C02-U278`                                                                     |
| `FC06-C02-U280` | **Definition — tangent sheaf, canonical sheaf, geometric genus**                                | §8 Differentials; pp. 172–189; L4138                      | For nonsingular n-fold X, T_X=Hom(Ω_{X/k},O_X), ω_X=∧^nΩ_{X/k}; if X is projective, p_g(X)=dim_k Γ(X,ω_X).                                                                                                                                                                          | `FC06-C02-U275`, `FC06-C02-U101`, `FC06-C02-U103`, `FC06-C02-U091`                  |
| `FC06-C02-U281` | **Remark 8.18.2 — arithmetic versus geometric genus**                                           | §8 Differentials; pp. 172–189; L4140                      | For smooth projective curves arithmetic and geometric genus coincide; in dimension ≥2 they need not.                                                                                                                                                                                | `FC06-C02-U280`                                                                     |
| `FC06-C02-U282` | **Remark 8.18.3 — geometric genus as intrinsic and birational data**                            | §8 Differentials; pp. 172–189; L4142                      | Differentials, tangent and canonical sheaves are intrinsic isomorphism invariants; the next theorem strengthens p_g to a birational invariant for nonsingular projective varieties.                                                                                                 | `FC06-C02-U280`                                                                     |
| `FC06-C02-U283` | **Theorem 8.19 — birational invariance of geometric genus**                                     | §8 Differentials; pp. 172–189; L4144–4154                 | Birationally equivalent nonsingular projective varieties over k have the same geometric genus.                                                                                                                                                                                      | `FC06-C02-U280`, `FC06-C02-U282`, `FC06-C02-U087`                                   |
| `FC06-C02-U284` | **Definition — conormal and normal sheaves**                                                    | §8 Differentials; pp. 172–189; L4156–4164                 | For nonsingular Y⊂X with ideal I, I/I² is the conormal sheaf and N_{Y/X}=Hom(I/I²,O_Y) its normal sheaf, locally free of rank codim(Y,X); dualizing the conormal sequence gives 0→T_Y→T_X\|*Y→N*{Y/X}→0.                                                                            | `FC06-C02-U277`, `FC06-C02-U101`, `FC06-C02-U280`                                   |
| `FC06-C02-U285` | **Proposition 8.20 — adjunction formula**                                                       | §8 Differentials; pp. 172–189; L4166–4174                 | For nonsingular codimension-r Y⊂X, ω_Y≅ω_X\|*Y⊗∧^r N*{Y/X}; for r=1 with associated divisor sheaf L, ω_Y≅ω_X⊗L⊗O_Y.                                                                                                                                                                 | `FC06-C02-U284`, `FC06-C02-U280`, `FC06-C02-U200`                                   |
| `FC06-C02-U286` | **Example 8.20.1 — canonical sheaf of projective space**                                        | §8 Differentials; pp. 172–189; L4176–4182                 | Dualizing the Euler sequence yields the tangent sequence; taking determinants gives ω_{P^n}=O(−n−1), hence p_g(P^n)=0 and every nonsingular projective rational variety has p_g=0 by birational invariance.                                                                         | `FC06-C02-U272`, `FC06-C02-U285`, `FC06-C02-U283`                                   |
| `FC06-C02-U287` | **Example 8.20.2 — existence of smooth hypersurfaces**                                          | §8 Differentials; pp. 172–189; L4184                      | For every d≥1 and n≥2, smooth degree-d hypersurfaces in P^n exist and form a dense open subset of the complete system \|dH\|.                                                                                                                                                       | `FC06-C02-U278`, `FC06-C02-U214`, `FC06-C02-U219`                                   |
| `FC06-C02-U288` | **Example 8.20.3 — canonical sheaf of a smooth hypersurface**                                   | §8 Differentials; pp. 172–189; L4186–4214                 | For a smooth degree-d hypersurface Y⊂P^n, adjunction gives ω_Y≅O_Y(d−n−1), yielding the usual genus/canonical calculations in low dimensions.                                                                                                                                       | `FC06-C02-U285`, `FC06-C02-U286`, `FC06-C02-U287`                                   |
| `FC06-C02-U289` | **Theorem 8.21A — Cohen–Macaulay and regular-sequence facts**                                   | §8 Differentials; pp. 172–189; L4216–4220                 | For local noetherian A: regular implies Cohen–Macaulay; CM localizes; in a CM ring x_1,…,x_r is regular iff quotient dimension drops by r; quotient by a regular sequence is CM; and for I=(x_i), gr_I A≅(A/I)[t_1,…,t_r], so I/I² is free and Sym^n(I/I²)≅I^n/I^{n+1}.             | `FC06-C02-U052`, `FC06-C01-U094`                                                    |
| `FC06-C02-U290` | **Theorem 8.22A — Serre criterion for normality**                                               | §8 Differentials; pp. 172–189; L4227–4231                 | A noetherian ring is normal iff it satisfies R_1 (localizations at primes of height ≤1 are regular) and S_2 (depth at primes of height ≥2 is at least 2, with the height-one consequence).                                                                                          | `FC06-C02-U151`, `FC06-C02-U289`                                                    |
| `FC06-C02-U291` | **Definition — local complete intersection**                                                    | §8 Differentials; pp. 172–189; L4235                      | A closed subscheme Y of a nonsingular variety X is a local complete intersection in X if its ideal sheaf is locally generated by r=codim(Y,X) elements.                                                                                                                             | `FC06-C02-U060`, `FC06-C02-U273`, `FC06-C02-U065`                                   |
| `FC06-C02-U292` | **Example 8.22.1 — nonsingular subvarieties are local complete intersections**                  | §8 Differentials; pp. 172–189; L4237                      | A nonsingular subvariety Y inside a nonsingular X is a local complete intersection.                                                                                                                                                                                                 | `FC06-C02-U277`, `FC06-C02-U291`                                                    |
| `FC06-C02-U293` | **Remark 8.22.2 — lci is intrinsic**                                                            | §8 Differentials; pp. 172–189; L4239                      | The local-complete-intersection property is intrinsic to Y, independent of the chosen embedding into a nonsingular ambient variety; Hartshorne notes this is proved using the cotangent complex.                                                                                    | `FC06-C02-U291`                                                                     |
| `FC06-C02-U294` | **Proposition 8.23 — local complete intersections are Cohen–Macaulay**                          | §8 Differentials; pp. 172–189; L4241–4249                 | If Y is lci in a nonsingular variety, then Y is Cohen–Macaulay; moreover Y is normal iff it is regular in codimension one.                                                                                                                                                          | `FC06-C02-U291`, `FC06-C02-U289`, `FC06-C02-U290`                                   |
| `FC06-C02-U295` | **Theorem 8.24 — blowup along a nonsingular center**                                            | §8 Differentials; pp. 172–189; L4253–4267                 | If X and closed Y⊂X are nonsingular and X~ is the blowup of I_Y, then X~ is nonsingular; the exceptional divisor Y′≅P(I/I²) over Y; under this identification its normal sheaf is O_{P(I/I²)}(−1).                                                                                  | `FC06-C02-U239`, `FC06-C02-U236`, `FC06-C02-U277`, `FC06-C02-U284`, `FC06-C02-U289` |
| `FC06-C02-U296` | **Theorem 8.25A — Cohen field of representatives**                                              | §8 Differentials; pp. 172–189; L4271–4273                 | If complete local A contains k and its residue field is separably generated over k, then A contains a subfield K⊇k mapping isomorphically onto the residue field; K is a field of representatives.                                                                                  | `FC06-C01-U099`, `FC06-C01-U079`                                                    |
| `FC06-C02-U297` | **Definition/construction — inverse systems and inverse limits of abelian groups**              | §9 Formal Schemes; pp. 190–200; L4380                     | An inverse system (A_n,φ_{n′n}) has compatible transition maps for n′≥n; lim←A_n consists of compatible sequences and is characterized by the universal property for compatible maps into all A_n.                                                                                  | —                                                                                   |
| `FC06-C02-U298` | **Construction/comparison — morphisms, exact systems, and left exactness of inverse limit**     | §9 Formal Schemes; pp. 190–200; L4384–4402                | Morphisms of inverse systems are levelwise maps commuting with transitions; exactness is levelwise. Applying inverse limit to a short exact sequence is always left exact, but the final map need not be surjective.                                                                | `FC06-C02-U297`                                                                     |
| `FC06-C02-U299` | **Definition — Mittag–Leffler condition**                                                       | §9 Formal Schemes; pp. 190–200; L4404                     | An inverse system satisfies (ML) when for each n the decreasing family of images im(A_{n′}→A_n) stabilizes for n′≫n.                                                                                                                                                                | `FC06-C02-U297`                                                                     |
| `FC06-C02-U300` | **Construction — stable-image inverse system**                                                  | §9 Formal Schemes; pp. 190–200; L4406                     | For an ML system, replacing each A_n by its stable image gives an inverse system with surjective transition maps and the same inverse limit; lim←A_n maps onto each stable image.                                                                                                   | `FC06-C02-U299`                                                                     |
| `FC06-C02-U301` | **Proposition 9.1 — Mittag–Leffler exactness criterion**                                        | §9 Formal Schemes; pp. 190–200; L4408–4422                | For a short exact sequence 0→(A_n)→(B_n)→(C_n)→0: if (B_n) is ML then (C_n) is ML; if (A_n) is ML, then 0→lim A_n→lim B_n→lim C_n→0 is exact.                                                                                                                                       | `FC06-C02-U298`, `FC06-C02-U299`                                                    |
| `FC06-C02-U302` | **Example 9.1.1 — surjective systems are Mittag–Leffler**                                       | §9 Formal Schemes; pp. 190–200; L4430                     | If all transition maps are surjective, the inverse system satisfies the ML condition.                                                                                                                                                                                               | `FC06-C02-U299`                                                                     |
| `FC06-C02-U303` | **Example 9.1.2 — finite-dimensional/DCC systems are Mittag–Leffler**                           | §9 Formal Schemes; pp. 190–200; L4432                     | Inverse systems of finite-dimensional vector spaces, more generally modules satisfying descending chain condition, satisfy ML.                                                                                                                                                      | `FC06-C02-U299`                                                                     |
| `FC06-C02-U304` | **Definition/construction — inverse limit in an arbitrary category**                            | §9 Formal Schemes; pp. 190–200; L4436                     | For an inverse system in a category C, lim←A_n is an object with compatible projections universal for compatible cones; when it exists it is unique up to unique isomorphism.                                                                                                       | `FC06-C02-U297`                                                                     |
| `FC06-C02-U305` | **Proposition 9.2 — inverse limits of sheaves**                                                 | §9 Formal Schemes; pp. 190–200; L4438                     | Inverse limits exist for sheaves of abelian groups on X, and sections commute with them: Γ(U,lim←F_n)=lim←Γ(U,F_n) for every open U.                                                                                                                                                | `FC06-C02-U304`, `FC06-C02-U004`                                                    |
| `FC06-C02-U306` | **Caution 9.2.1 — inverse-limit exactness differs for sheaves**                                 | §9 Formal Schemes; pp. 190–200; L4442                     | The ML surjectivity conclusion of Proposition 9.1(b) can fail inside the category of sheaves even for surjective transition maps; exactness arguments therefore pass to sections.                                                                                                   | `FC06-C02-U305`, `FC06-C02-U301`                                                    |
| `FC06-C02-U307` | **Definition — I-adic completion of a ring**                                                    | §9 Formal Schemes; pp. 190–200; L4454                     | For ideal I⊆A, the inverse system A/I^n has completion Â=lim←A/I^n with its canonical homomorphism A→Â.                                                                                                                                                                             | `FC06-C02-U297`                                                                     |
| `FC06-C02-U308` | **Definition — I-adic completion of a module**                                                  | §9 Formal Schemes; pp. 190–200; L4456                     | For A-module M, M^=lim←M/I^nM, naturally an Â-module.                                                                                                                                                                                                                               | `FC06-C02-U307`                                                                     |
| `FC06-C02-U309` | **Theorem 9.3A — algebra of I-adic completion**                                                 | §9 Formal Schemes; pp. 190–200; L4458–4466                | For noetherian A: Î is an ideal of Â with Î^n=I^nÂ and Â/Î^n≅A/I^n; finite M satisfies M^≅M⊗_AÂ; completion is exact on finite modules; Â is noetherian; compatible surjective finite A/I^n-modules arise from a finite Â-module by inverse limit.                                  | `FC06-C02-U307`, `FC06-C02-U308`, `FC06-C02-U052`                                   |
| `FC06-C02-U310` | **Definition — formal completion of a scheme along a closed subscheme**                         | §9 Formal Schemes; pp. 190–200; L4473                     | For noetherian X and closed Y defined by I, the formal completion X^ has underlying topological space Y and structure sheaf O_{X^}=lim←O_X/I^n.                                                                                                                                     | `FC06-C02-U052`, `FC06-C02-U060`, `FC06-C02-U305`                                   |
| `FC06-C02-U311` | **Remark 9.3.1 — formal completion depends only on the closed support**                         | §9 Formal Schemes; pp. 190–200; L4475–4477                | O_{X^} depends only on the closed subset Y, not its chosen closed-subscheme structure, because ideals defining the same closed support have cofinal powers; on affine U=Spec A its sections are the I-adic completion of A.                                                         | `FC06-C02-U310`, `FC06-C02-U307`                                                    |
| `FC06-C02-U312` | **Definition — completion of a coherent sheaf**                                                 | §9 Formal Schemes; pp. 190–200; L4479                     | For coherent F on X and Y defined by I, define F^=lim←F/I^nF on Y; it is naturally an O_{X^}-module.                                                                                                                                                                                | `FC06-C02-U310`, `FC06-C02-U111`, `FC06-C02-U305`                                   |
| `FC06-C02-U313` | **Definition — noetherian formal scheme, morphism, coherent sheaf**                             | §9 Formal Schemes; pp. 190–200; L4481                     | A noetherian formal scheme is a locally ringed space with a finite open cover by completions of noetherian schemes along closed subschemes; morphisms are locally-ringed-space morphisms, and coherent modules are locally completions of coherent modules on such algebraizations. | `FC06-C02-U310`, `FC06-C02-U312`, `FC06-C02-U028`                                   |
| `FC06-C02-U314` | **Examples 9.3.2 — algebraizable and nonalgebraizable formal schemes**                          | §9 Formal Schemes; pp. 190–200; L4483                     | A completion of one noetherian scheme along one closed subscheme is algebraizable; nonalgebraizable noetherian formal schemes also exist.                                                                                                                                           | `FC06-C02-U313`                                                                     |
| `FC06-C02-U315` | **Example 9.3.3 — schemes as formal schemes**                                                   | §9 Formal Schemes; pp. 190–200; L4485                     | Completing a noetherian scheme X along Y=X gives X itself, so noetherian schemes form a subcategory of noetherian formal schemes.                                                                                                                                                   | `FC06-C02-U310`, `FC06-C02-U313`                                                    |
| `FC06-C02-U316` | **Example 9.3.4 — completion at a closed point**                                                | §9 Formal Schemes; pp. 190–200; L4487–4489                | Completing noetherian X at a closed point P gives a one-point formal scheme with structure ring the completed local ring O^_{X,P}; its coherent sheaves are exactly finite modules over that ring.                                                                                  | `FC06-C02-U310`, `FC06-C02-U307`, `FC06-C02-U008`, `FC06-C02-U313`                  |
| `FC06-C02-U317` | **Definition — affine formal scheme and M^Δ**                                                   | §9 Formal Schemes; pp. 190–200; L4491                     | An affine formal scheme is the completion of an affine noetherian scheme Spec A along V(I); for finite A-module M, M^Δ is the completion of M~ and is coherent.                                                                                                                     | `FC06-C02-U310`, `FC06-C02-U108`, `FC06-C02-U312`                                   |
| `FC06-C02-U318` | **Proposition 9.4 — basic affine formal-scheme module properties**                              | §9 Formal Schemes; pp. 190–200; L4493–4521                | For X^ the completion of Spec A along V(I): I^Δ is an ideal with O_{X^}/(I^Δ)^n≅(A/I^n)~; M^Δ≅M~⊗*{O_X}O*{X^}; and M↦M^Δ is exact on finite A-modules.                                                                                                                              | `FC06-C02-U317`, `FC06-C02-U309`, `FC06-C02-U102`                                   |
| `FC06-C02-U319` | **Definition — ideal of definition**                                                            | §9 Formal Schemes; pp. 190–200; L4523                     | An ideal sheaf J⊆O_𝔛 on a noetherian formal scheme is an ideal of definition if Supp(O_𝔛/J)=𝔛 and (𝔛,O_𝔛/J) is a noetherian scheme.                                                                                                                                                 | `FC06-C02-U313`, `FC06-C02-U104`                                                    |
| `FC06-C02-U320` | **Proposition 9.5 — properties of ideals of definition**                                        | §9 Formal Schemes; pp. 190–200; L4525–4539                | Any two ideals of definition contain powers of one another; there is a unique largest ideal of definition, whose quotient scheme is reduced; and every positive power of an ideal of definition is again one.                                                                       | `FC06-C02-U319`, `FC06-C02-U048`                                                    |
| `FC06-C02-U321` | **Proposition 9.6 — coherent formal sheaves as compatible infinitesimal layers**                | §9 Formal Schemes; pp. 190–200; L4541–4551                | For ideal of definition J and Y_n=(𝔛,O_𝔛/J^n), a coherent F gives coherent F_n=F/J^nF with F≅lim←F_n; conversely a compatible surjective system of coherent F_n with the expected kernels has coherent inverse limit and recovers each F_n by quotient.                             | `FC06-C02-U319`, `FC06-C02-U305`, `FC06-C02-U313`                                   |
| `FC06-C02-U322` | **Theorem 9.7 — affine formal schemes and finite modules**                                      | §9 Formal Schemes; pp. 190–200; L4553–4577                | If noetherian A is I-adically complete and 𝔛 is the completion of Spec A along V(I), M↦M^Δ and Γ(𝔛,−) are exact inverse equivalences between finite A-modules and coherent O_𝔛-modules.                                                                                             | `FC06-C02-U317`, `FC06-C02-U309`, `FC06-C02-U321`                                   |
| `FC06-C02-U323` | **Corollary 9.8 — exactness and tensor description of sheaf completion**                        | §9 Formal Schemes; pp. 190–200; L4579–4581                | For completion X^ of noetherian X along Y, F↦F^ is exact on coherent sheaves; F^/(I^)^nF^≅F/I^nF and F^ is obtained by tensoring with O_{X^}.                                                                                                                                       | `FC06-C02-U312`, `FC06-C02-U322`, `FC06-C02-U318`                                   |
| `FC06-C02-U324` | **Corollary 9.9 — kernels, cokernels, and images remain coherent**                              | §9 Formal Schemes; pp. 190–200; L4583–4585                | On a noetherian formal scheme, kernels, cokernels, and images of morphisms of coherent sheaves are coherent.                                                                                                                                                                        | `FC06-C02-U323`, `FC06-C02-U100`                                                    |
| `FC06-C02-U325` | **Remark 9.9.1 — extensions and failure of global generation on formal schemes**                | §9 Formal Schemes; pp. 190–200; L4587                     | Extensions of coherent formal-scheme sheaves are coherent, but scheme-theoretic generation properties can fail: a nonzero coherent sheaf on a projective completion may have no global sections after any twist.                                                                    | `FC06-C02-U324`, `FC06-C02-U142`, `FC06-C02-U130`                                   |

## Hartshorne, *Algebraic Geometry* — Chapter III: Cohomology

| ID              | Kind / source unit                                                                                    | Location                                                             | Concise data                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                             | Earlier FC06 dependencies                                                           |
| --------------- | ----------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- | -------------------------------------------------------------------- | ------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------ | ----------------------------------------------------------------------------------- |
| `FC06-C03-U001` | **Definition — abelian category**                                                                     | §1 Derived Functors; pp. 203–206; L4679                              | An abelian category has abelian Hom groups with bilinear composition, finite direct sums, kernels/cokernels, every mono/epi is normal, and every morphism factors as an epi followed by a mono.                                                                          | —                                                                                   |
| `FC06-C03-U002` | **Example 1.0.1 — abelian groups**                                                                    | §1 Derived Functors; pp. 203–206; L4683                              | The category Ab of abelian groups is abelian.                                                                                                                                                                                                                            | `FC06-C03-U001`                                                                     |
| `FC06-C03-U003` | **Example 1.0.2 — modules**                                                                           | §1 Derived Functors; pp. 203–206; L4685                              | For a ring A, Mod(A) is an abelian category.                                                                                                                                                                                                                             | `FC06-C03-U001`                                                                     |
| `FC06-C03-U004` | **Example 1.0.3 — sheaves of abelian groups**                                                         | §1 Derived Functors; pp. 203–206; L4687                              | For a topological space X, Ab(X), the category of sheaves of abelian groups, is abelian.                                                                                                                                                                                 | `FC06-C03-U001`, `FC06-C02-U004`                                                    |
| `FC06-C03-U005` | **Example 1.0.4 — sheaves of modules**                                                                | §1 Derived Functors; pp. 203–206; L4689                              | For a ringed space X, Mod(X), the category of O_X-modules, is abelian.                                                                                                                                                                                                   | `FC06-C03-U001`, `FC06-C02-U099`                                                    |
| `FC06-C03-U006` | **Example 1.0.5 — quasi-coherent sheaves**                                                            | §1 Derived Functors; pp. 203–206; L4691                              | For a scheme X, quasi-coherent O_X-modules form an abelian category.                                                                                                                                                                                                     | `FC06-C03-U001`, `FC06-C02-U122`                                                    |
| `FC06-C03-U007` | **Example 1.0.6 — coherent sheaves**                                                                  | §1 Derived Functors; pp. 203–206; L4693                              | On a noetherian scheme, coherent O_X-modules form an abelian category.                                                                                                                                                                                                   | `FC06-C03-U001`, `FC06-C02-U122`, `FC06-C02-U052`                                   |
| `FC06-C03-U008` | **Example 1.0.7 — coherent sheaves on noetherian formal schemes**                                     | §1 Derived Functors; pp. 203–206; L4695                              | Coherent modules on a noetherian formal scheme form an abelian category.                                                                                                                                                                                                 | `FC06-C03-U001`, `FC06-C02-U324`                                                    |
| `FC06-C03-U009` | **Definition/construction — complexes, cohomology objects, homotopies**                               | §1 Derived Functors; pp. 203–206; L4699–4707                         | A complex A^• has d²=0; h^i(A^•)=ker d^i/im d^{i−1}. Morphisms induce cohomology maps, short exact sequences of complexes induce long exact cohomology sequences, and homotopic maps induce the same maps on cohomology.                                                 | `FC06-C03-U001`                                                                     |
| `FC06-C03-U010` | **Definition — additive, left/right exact, exact functors**                                           | §1 Derived Functors; pp. 203–206; L4709–4729                         | An additive functor preserves addition on Hom groups; left/right exactness means preservation of the corresponding half of every short exact sequence, and exact means both. Contravariant left exactness reverses the short sequence.                                   | `FC06-C03-U001`                                                                     |
| `FC06-C03-U011` | **Example 1.0.8 — Hom functors are left exact**                                                       | §1 Derived Functors; pp. 203–206; L4731                              | For fixed A in an abelian category, Hom(A,−) is covariant left exact and Hom(−,A) is contravariant left exact.                                                                                                                                                           | `FC06-C03-U010`                                                                     |
| `FC06-C03-U012` | **Definition/construction — injectives, enough injectives, injective resolutions**                    | §1 Derived Functors; pp. 203–206; L4733–4741                         | I is injective when Hom(−,I) is exact. An injective resolution 0→A→I^0→I^1→… has all I^i injective. If the category has enough injectives, every object admits such a resolution, unique up to homotopy equivalence.                                                     | `FC06-C03-U011`, `FC06-C03-U009`                                                    |
| `FC06-C03-U013` | **Definition/construction — right derived functors**                                                  | §1 Derived Functors; pp. 203–206; L4743                              | For left exact F on an abelian category with enough injectives, choose an injective resolution I^• of A and define R^iF(A)=h^i(F(I^•)).                                                                                                                                  | `FC06-C03-U012`, `FC06-C03-U010`, `FC06-C03-U009`                                   |
| `FC06-C03-U014` | **Theorem 1.1A — basic properties of right derived functors**                                         | §1 Derived Functors; pp. 203–206; L4745–4757                         | R^iF are well-defined additive functors independent up to natural isomorphism of resolutions; R^0F≅F; short exact sequences yield natural long exact sequences; morphisms of short exact sequences give compatible connecting morphisms.                                 | `FC06-C03-U013`                                                                     |
| `FC06-C03-U015` | **Definition — F-acyclic object**                                                                     | §1 Derived Functors; pp. 203–206; L4759                              | An object J is acyclic for F if R^iF(J)=0 for all i>0.                                                                                                                                                                                                                   | `FC06-C03-U013`                                                                     |
| `FC06-C03-U016` | **Proposition 1.2A — computing derived functors from acyclic resolutions**                            | §1 Derived Functors; pp. 203–206; L4761–4771                         | If 0→A→J^0→J^1→… is an F-acyclic resolution, then R^iF(A)≅h^i(F(J^•)) for all i≥0.                                                                                                                                                                                       | `FC06-C03-U015`, `FC06-C03-U009`, `FC06-C03-U013`                                   |
| `FC06-C03-U017` | **Definition — δ-functor**                                                                            | §1 Derived Functors; pp. 203–206; L4773–4785                         | A covariant δ-functor T=(T^i) consists of functors with natural connecting maps for short exact sequences, producing functorial long exact sequences.                                                                                                                    | `FC06-C03-U001`                                                                     |
| `FC06-C03-U018` | **Definition — universal δ-functor**                                                                  | §1 Derived Functors; pp. 203–206; L4787                              | A δ-functor T is universal if any morphism T^0→T′^0 extends uniquely to a morphism of δ-functors T→T′.                                                                                                                                                                   | `FC06-C03-U017`                                                                     |
| `FC06-C03-U019` | **Remark 1.2.1 — uniqueness of universal δ-functors**                                                 | §1 Derived Functors; pp. 203–206; L4789                              | For fixed additive F there is at most one universal δ-functor with T^0=F up to unique isomorphism; its higher terms are the right satellite functors.                                                                                                                    | `FC06-C03-U018`                                                                     |
| `FC06-C03-U020` | **Definition — effaceable and coeffaceable functors**                                                 | §1 Derived Functors; pp. 203–206; L4791                              | An additive F is effaceable if every object A embeds into some M with F(A→M)=0; coeffaceable dually uses an epimorphism P→A killed by F.                                                                                                                                 | `FC06-C03-U010`                                                                     |
| `FC06-C03-U021` | **Theorem 1.3A — effaceable δ-functors are universal**                                                | §1 Derived Functors; pp. 203–206; L4793–4795                         | If T is a covariant δ-functor and every T^i for i>0 is effaceable, then T is universal.                                                                                                                                                                                  | `FC06-C03-U017`, `FC06-C03-U020`                                                    |
| `FC06-C03-U022` | **Corollary 1.4 — derived functors are the universal δ-functor**                                      | §1 Derived Functors; pp. 203–206; L4797–4801                         | With enough injectives, (R^iF) is the universal δ-functor extending left exact F. Conversely every universal δ-functor is naturally isomorphic to the derived functors of its degree-zero term.                                                                          | `FC06-C03-U014`, `FC06-C03-U021`, `FC06-C03-U018`                                   |
| `FC06-C03-U023` | **Proposition 2.1A — enough injective modules**                                                       | §2 Cohomology of Sheaves; pp. 207–214; L4807                         | Every module over any ring embeds in an injective module.                                                                                                                                                                                                                | `FC06-C03-U003`                                                                     |
| `FC06-C03-U024` | **Proposition 2.2 — sheaves of modules have enough injectives**                                       | §2 Cohomology of Sheaves; pp. 207–214; L4811–4815                    | For any ringed space X, Mod(X) has enough injectives.                                                                                                                                                                                                                    | `FC06-C03-U005`, `FC06-C03-U023`                                                    |
| `FC06-C03-U025` | **Corollary 2.3 — abelian sheaves have enough injectives**                                            | §2 Cohomology of Sheaves; pp. 207–214; L4817                         | For any topological space X, Ab(X) has enough injectives.                                                                                                                                                                                                                | `FC06-C03-U004`, `FC06-C03-U024`                                                    |
| `FC06-C03-U026` | **Definition — sheaf cohomology**                                                                     | §2 Cohomology of Sheaves; pp. 207–214; L4821                         | For a topological space X, H^i(X,−) is the ith right derived functor of Γ(X,−) on abelian sheaves. All scheme/sheaf cohomology is taken in this underlying abelian-sheaf sense.                                                                                          | `FC06-C03-U013`, `FC06-C03-U025`, `FC06-C02-U003`                                   |
| `FC06-C03-U027` | **Definition — flasque sheaf**                                                                        | §2 Cohomology of Sheaves; pp. 207–214; L4823                         | A sheaf F is flasque if every restriction F(U)→F(V) for V⊆U is surjective.                                                                                                                                                                                               | `FC06-C02-U004`                                                                     |
| `FC06-C03-U028` | **Lemma 2.4 — injective module sheaves are flasque**                                                  | §2 Cohomology of Sheaves; pp. 207–214; L4827                         | Every injective O_X-module on a ringed space is flasque.                                                                                                                                                                                                                 | `FC06-C03-U027`, `FC06-C03-U024`                                                    |
| `FC06-C03-U029` | **Proposition 2.5 — flasque sheaves are acyclic for global sections**                                 | §2 Cohomology of Sheaves; pp. 207–214; L4831–4845                    | If F is flasque, then H^i(X,F)=0 for all i>0.                                                                                                                                                                                                                            | `FC06-C03-U027`, `FC06-C03-U026`                                                    |
| `FC06-C03-U030` | **Remark 2.5.1 — cohomology from flasque resolutions**                                                | §2 Cohomology of Sheaves; pp. 207–214; L4847                         | Flasque sheaves are Γ-acyclic, so sheaf cohomology may be computed using flasque resolutions.                                                                                                                                                                            | `FC06-C03-U029`, `FC06-C03-U016`                                                    |
| `FC06-C03-U031` | **Proposition 2.6 — module-derived global sections equal sheaf cohomology**                           | §2 Cohomology of Sheaves; pp. 207–214; L4849–4851                    | For a ringed space, the derived functors of Γ(X,−) on O_X-modules coincide with H^i(X,−) defined in abelian sheaves.                                                                                                                                                     | `FC06-C03-U026`, `FC06-C03-U024`, `FC06-C03-U028`                                   |
| `FC06-C03-U032` | **Remark 2.6.1 — module structure on cohomology**                                                     | §2 Cohomology of Sheaves; pp. 207–214; L4853–4855                    | If A=Γ(X,O_X), then H^i(X,F) for an O_X-module F is naturally an A-module and all long exact sequences are A-linear; for X over Spec B they are B-modules.                                                                                                               | `FC06-C03-U031`, `FC06-C02-U003`                                                    |
| `FC06-C03-U033` | **Theorem 2.7 — Grothendieck vanishing**                                                              | §2 Cohomology of Sheaves; pp. 207–214; L4857–4859                    | If X is a noetherian topological space of dimension n, then H^i(X,F)=0 for every abelian sheaf F and every i>n.                                                                                                                                                          | `FC06-C03-U026`, `FC06-C01-U027`, `FC06-C01-U023`                                   |
| `FC06-C03-U034` | **Lemma 2.8 — direct limits of flasque sheaves**                                                      | §2 Cohomology of Sheaves; pp. 207–214; L4861–4875                    | On a noetherian topological space, a direct limit of flasque sheaves is flasque.                                                                                                                                                                                         | `FC06-C03-U027`, `FC06-C01-U023`                                                    |
| `FC06-C03-U035` | **Proposition 2.9 — cohomology commutes with direct limits**                                          | §2 Cohomology of Sheaves; pp. 207–214; L4877–4891                    | On a noetherian topological space, H^i(X,lim→F_α)≅lim→H^i(X,F_α) naturally for every i≥0.                                                                                                                                                                                | `FC06-C03-U034`, `FC06-C03-U030`                                                    |
| `FC06-C03-U036` | **Remark 2.9.1 — cohomology commutes with arbitrary direct sums**                                     | §2 Cohomology of Sheaves; pp. 207–214; L4893                         | As a special case of direct-limit compatibility, sheaf cohomology commutes with infinite direct sums.                                                                                                                                                                    | `FC06-C03-U035`                                                                     |
| `FC06-C03-U037` | **Lemma 2.10 — cohomology under extension by zero from a closed subset**                              | §2 Cohomology of Sheaves; pp. 207–214; L4895                         | For closed j:Y↪X and abelian sheaf F on Y, H^i(Y,F)≅H^i(X,j_*F) for all i.                                                                                                                                                                                               | `FC06-C03-U026`, `FC06-C02-U105`                                                    |
| `FC06-C03-U038` | **Remark 2.10.1 — suppressing closed extension by zero**                                              | §2 Cohomology of Sheaves; pp. 207–214; L4899                         | Hartshorne often writes F for j_*F when a sheaf on a closed subset is regarded on X; Lemma 2.10 makes its cohomology unambiguous.                                                                                                                                        | `FC06-C03-U037`                                                                     |
| `FC06-C03-U039` | **Proposition 3.1A — Krull topology theorem**                                                         | §3 Cohomology of a Noetherian Affine Scheme; pp. 215–217; L5015      | For noetherian A, finite M⊆N, and ideal a, the a-adic topology on M is induced from N: for each n there is n′≥n with a^nM⊇M∩a^{n′}N.                                                                                                                                     | `FC06-C02-U052`                                                                     |
