| `FC06-C03-U040` | **Construction — Γ_a(M)**                                                                             | §3 Cohomology of a Noetherian Affine Scheme; pp. 215–217; L5017      | For ideal a⊆A and A-module M, Γ_a(M)={m∈M : a^n m=0 for some n>0}.                                                                                                                                                                                                       | —                                                                                   |
| `FC06-C03-U041` | **Lemma 3.2 — torsion submodule of an injective is injective**                                        | §3 Cohomology of a Noetherian Affine Scheme; pp. 215–217; L5019–5025 | If A is noetherian, a⊆A, and I is injective, then Γ_a(I) is injective.                                                                                                                                                                                                   | `FC06-C03-U040`, `FC06-C03-U023`, `FC06-C03-U039`                                   |
| `FC06-C03-U042` | **Lemma 3.3 — localization of an injective module is a quotient**                                     | §3 Cohomology of a Noetherian Affine Scheme; pp. 215–217; L5027      | For injective I over noetherian A and f∈A, the natural map I→I_f is surjective.                                                                                                                                                                                          | `FC06-C03-U041`                                                                     |
| `FC06-C03-U043` | **Proposition 3.4 — sheaf of an injective module is flasque**                                         | §3 Cohomology of a Noetherian Affine Scheme; pp. 215–217; L5031–5043 | If I is injective over noetherian A, then I~ on Spec A is flasque.                                                                                                                                                                                                       | `FC06-C03-U042`, `FC06-C02-U108`, `FC06-C03-U027`                                   |
| `FC06-C03-U044` | **Theorem 3.5 — vanishing on noetherian affine schemes**                                              | §3 Cohomology of a Noetherian Affine Scheme; pp. 215–217; L5045      | If X=Spec A with A noetherian and F quasi-coherent, then H^i(X,F)=0 for every i>0.                                                                                                                                                                                       | `FC06-C03-U043`, `FC06-C03-U029`, `FC06-C02-U119`                                   |
| `FC06-C03-U045` | **Remark 3.5.1 — noetherian hypothesis can be removed**                                               | §3 Cohomology of a Noetherian Affine Scheme; pp. 215–217; L5049      | The affine quasi-coherent vanishing theorem remains true without the noetherian hypothesis, though Hartshorne does not prove that generality here.                                                                                                                       | `FC06-C03-U044`                                                                     |
| `FC06-C03-U046` | **Corollary 3.6 — quasi-coherent sheaves embed in flasque quasi-coherent sheaves**                    | §3 Cohomology of a Noetherian Affine Scheme; pp. 215–217; L5051      | On a noetherian scheme every quasi-coherent F embeds in a flasque quasi-coherent G.                                                                                                                                                                                      | `FC06-C03-U044`, `FC06-C03-U043`, `FC06-C02-U111`                                   |
| `FC06-C03-U047` | **Theorem 3.7 — Serre cohomological criterion for affineness**                                        | §3 Cohomology of a Noetherian Affine Scheme; pp. 215–217; L5055–5087 | For noetherian X, the following are equivalent: X affine; H^i(X,F)=0 for every quasi-coherent F and i>0; H^1(X,I)=0 for every coherent ideal sheaf I.                                                                                                                    | `FC06-C03-U044`, `FC06-C02-U104`, `FC06-C02-U111`                                   |
| `FC06-C03-U048` | **Remark 3.7.1 — Stein-space analogy**                                                                | §3 Cohomology of a Noetherian Affine Scheme; pp. 215–217; L5089      | Serre’s affine criterion is analogous to the characterization of Stein spaces by vanishing of coherent analytic sheaf cohomology.                                                                                                                                        | `FC06-C03-U047`                                                                     |
| `FC06-C03-U049` | **Construction — Čech complex for an open cover**                                                     | §4 Čech Cohomology; pp. 218–224; L5146–5166                          | For a well-ordered open cover U=(U_i) and abelian sheaf F, set C^p(U,F)=∏*{i_0<⋯<i_p}F(U*{i_0…i_p}) with the alternating restriction coboundary; d²=0.                                                                                                                   | `FC06-C03-U009`, `FC06-C02-U004`                                                    |
| `FC06-C03-U050` | **Remark 4.0.1 — alternating-index convention**                                                       | §4 Čech Cohomology; pp. 218–224; L5168                               | Extend a Čech cochain component to arbitrary index tuples by zero on repetitions and the sign of permutations; the usual differential formula then applies without ordering hypotheses.                                                                                  | `FC06-C03-U049`                                                                     |
| `FC06-C03-U051` | **Definition — Čech cohomology**                                                                      | §4 Čech Cohomology; pp. 218–224; L5170                               | Define Čech H^p(U,F)=h^p(C^•(U,F)).                                                                                                                                                                                                                                      | `FC06-C03-U049`                                                                     |
| `FC06-C03-U052` | **Caution 4.0.2 — fixed-cover Čech cohomology is not generally a δ-functor**                          | §4 Čech Cohomology; pp. 218–224; L5172–5176                          | For a fixed cover, a short exact sequence of sheaves need not induce a long exact Čech sequence; even the one-open-set cover reflects nonexactness of global sections.                                                                                                   | `FC06-C03-U051`, `FC06-C03-U017`                                                    |
| `FC06-C03-U053` | **Example 4.0.3 — Čech computation on P¹**                                                            | §4 Čech Cohomology; pp. 218–224; L5178–5214                          | For P¹ covered by its two standard affine lines, the Čech complex computes cohomology of Ω_{P¹}; Hartshorne exhibits the two-term section complex and its quotient explicitly.                                                                                           | `FC06-C03-U051`, `FC06-C02-U265`                                                    |
| `FC06-C03-U054` | **Example 4.0.4 — constant sheaf on the circle**                                                      | §4 Čech Cohomology; pp. 218–224; L5216–5224                          | For S¹ covered by two connected semicircles with two-component overlap and constant Z, the two-term Čech complex gives Čech H^0≅Z and H^1≅Z.                                                                                                                             | `FC06-C03-U051`                                                                     |
| `FC06-C03-U055` | **Lemma 4.1 — Čech degree zero equals global sections**                                               | §4 Čech Cohomology; pp. 218–224; L5226–5242                          | For every X, open cover U, and sheaf F, Čech H^0(U,F)≅Γ(X,F).                                                                                                                                                                                                            | `FC06-C03-U051`                                                                     |
| `FC06-C03-U056` | **Lemma 4.2 — Čech sheaf complex resolves F**                                                         | §4 Čech Cohomology; pp. 218–224; L5244–5272                          | The sheafified Čech complex C^•(U,F), augmented by F→C^0(U,F), is an exact resolution of F.                                                                                                                                                                              | `FC06-C03-U049`                                                                     |
| `FC06-C03-U057` | **Proposition 4.3 — higher Čech cohomology of flasque sheaves vanishes**                              | §4 Čech Cohomology; pp. 218–224; L5274–5282                          | If F is flasque, Čech H^p(U,F)=0 for every p>0 and every open cover U.                                                                                                                                                                                                   | `FC06-C03-U027`, `FC06-C03-U051`                                                    |
| `FC06-C03-U058` | **Lemma 4.4 — natural comparison map**                                                                | §4 Čech Cohomology; pp. 218–224; L5284–5290                          | For every cover U and sheaf F there are natural functorial maps Čech H^p(U,F)→H^p(X,F).                                                                                                                                                                                  | `FC06-C03-U056`, `FC06-C03-U057`, `FC06-C03-U026`                                   |
| `FC06-C03-U059` | **Theorem 4.5 — Čech computes quasi-coherent cohomology on separated noetherian schemes**             | §4 Čech Cohomology; pp. 218–224; L5292–5334                          | If X is noetherian separated, U is an affine open cover, and F is quasi-coherent, then Čech H^p(U,F)→H^p(X,F) is an isomorphism for all p≥0.                                                                                                                             | `FC06-C03-U058`, `FC06-C03-U044`, `FC06-C02-U078`, `FC06-C02-U111`                  |
| `FC06-C03-U060` | **Construction — graded cohomology module on projective space**                                       | §5 The Cohomology of Projective Space; pp. 225–232; L5456            | For noetherian A, S=A[x_0,…,x_r], X=P^r_A, and any O_X-module F, set Γ_*(F)=⊕_{n∈Z}Γ(X,F(n)), a graded S-module.                                                                                                                                                         | `FC06-C02-U044`, `FC06-C02-U130`                                                    |
| `FC06-C03-U061` | **Theorem 5.1 — cohomology of O(n) on projective space**                                              | §5 The Cohomology of Projective Space; pp. 225–232; L5458            | For X=P^r_A, r≥1: Γ_*(O_X)≅S; H^i(X,O_X(n))=0 for 0<i<r; H^r(X,O_X(−r−1))≅A; and multiplication gives perfect pairings H^0(O(n))×H^r(O(−n−r−1))→A.                                                                                                                       | `FC06-C03-U060`, `FC06-C03-U059`, `FC06-C03-U036`                                   |
| `FC06-C03-U062` | **Theorem 5.2 — Serre finiteness and vanishing**                                                      | §5 The Cohomology of Projective Space; pp. 225–232; L5552            | If X is projective over noetherian A, O_X(1) is very ample, and F coherent, then every H^i(X,F) is finite over A and H^i(X,F(n))=0 for all i>0 and n≫0.                                                                                                                  | `FC06-C03-U061`, `FC06-C02-U091`, `FC06-C02-U140`, `FC06-C02-U146`                  |
| `FC06-C03-U063` | **Remark 5.2.1 — finiteness of global sections**                                                      | §5 The Cohomology of Projective Space; pp. 225–232; L5584            | The case i=0 of Serre finiteness says Γ(X,F) is a finite A-module for coherent F, recovering II.5.19.                                                                                                                                                                    | `FC06-C03-U062`, `FC06-C02-U147`                                                    |
| `FC06-C03-U064` | **Proposition 5.3 — cohomological criterion for ampleness**                                           | §5 The Cohomology of Projective Space; pp. 225–232; L5588            | For X proper over noetherian A and invertible L, L is ample iff for every coherent F there is n_0 with H^i(X,F⊗L^n)=0 for every i>0 and n≥n_0.                                                                                                                           | `FC06-C03-U062`, `FC06-C02-U207`, `FC06-C02-U087`                                   |
| `FC06-C03-U065` | **Definition — global Ext and sheaf Ext**                                                             | §6 Ext Groups and Sheaves; pp. 233–238; L5767                        | For a ringed space X and O_X-module F, Ext^i(F,−) is the right derived functor of Hom(F,−), and sheaf Ext^i(F,−) is the right derived functor of internal Hom(F,−).                                                                                                      | `FC06-C03-U013`, `FC06-C02-U101`, `FC06-C02-U099`                                   |
| `FC06-C03-U066` | **Lemma 6.1 — restriction preserves injectives**                                                      | §6 Ext Groups and Sheaves; pp. 233–238; L5771                        | If I is injective in Mod(X), then I\|_U is injective in Mod(U) for every open U⊆X.                                                                                                                                                                                       | `FC06-C03-U012`, `FC06-C02-U020`                                                    |
| `FC06-C03-U067` | **Proposition 6.2 — sheaf Ext is local**                                                              | §6 Ext Groups and Sheaves; pp. 233–238; L5775                        | For open U⊆X, sheaf Ext_X^i(F,G)\|_U≅sheaf Ext_U^i(F\|_U,G\|_U) naturally.                                                                                                                                                                                               | `FC06-C03-U065`, `FC06-C03-U066`                                                    |
| `FC06-C03-U068` | **Proposition 6.3 — Ext from O_X**                                                                    | §6 Ext Groups and Sheaves; pp. 233–238; L5783                        | For every O_X-module G, sheaf Ext^0(O_X,G)=G, sheaf Ext^i(O_X,G)=0 for i>0, and global Ext^i(O_X,G)≅H^i(X,G).                                                                                                                                                            | `FC06-C03-U065`, `FC06-C03-U031`                                                    |
| `FC06-C03-U069` | **Proposition 6.4 — long exact Ext sequence in the first variable**                                   | §6 Ext Groups and Sheaves; pp. 233–238; L5789                        | A short exact sequence 0→F′→F→F″→0 induces long exact sequences Ext^i(F″,G)→Ext^i(F,G)→Ext^i(F′,G)→… and likewise for sheaf Ext.                                                                                                                                         | `FC06-C03-U065`, `FC06-C03-U066`                                                    |
| `FC06-C03-U070` | **Proposition 6.5 — computing sheaf Ext from a locally free resolution**                              | §6 Ext Groups and Sheaves; pp. 233–238; L5807                        | If L_•→F is a resolution by locally free finite-rank sheaves, then sheaf Ext^i(F,G)≅h^i(Hom(L_•,G)).                                                                                                                                                                     | `FC06-C03-U065`, `FC06-C03-U009`, `FC06-C02-U103`                                   |
| `FC06-C03-U071` | **Example 6.5.1 — coherent sheaves on quasi-projective schemes admit locally free resolutions**       | §6 Ext Groups and Sheaves; pp. 233–238; L5821                        | On a scheme quasi-projective over a noetherian affine base, every coherent sheaf is a quotient of a locally free sheaf and hence admits a locally free resolution usable in Proposition 6.5.                                                                             | `FC06-C03-U070`, `FC06-C02-U146`                                                    |
| `FC06-C03-U072` | **Caution 6.5.2 — no general projective-resolution derived functor on Mod(X)**                        | §6 Ext Groups and Sheaves; pp. 233–238; L5823                        | The preceding exactness/computation does not make sheaf Ext a right derived functor in its first variable: Mod(X) generally does not have enough projectives.                                                                                                            | `FC06-C03-U070`, `FC06-C03-U069`                                                    |
| `FC06-C03-U073` | **Lemma 6.6 — tensoring an injective by a vector bundle**                                             | §6 Ext Groups and Sheaves; pp. 233–238; L5825                        | If L is locally free of finite rank and I injective, then L⊗I is injective.                                                                                                                                                                                              | `FC06-C02-U103`, `FC06-C02-U102`, `FC06-C03-U012`                                   |
| `FC06-C03-U074` | **Proposition 6.7 — Ext and locally free twists**                                                     | §6 Ext Groups and Sheaves; pp. 233–238; L5829                        | For locally free finite-rank L with dual L∨, Ext^i(F⊗L,G)≅Ext^i(F,L∨⊗G); for sheaf Ext this also identifies with sheaf Ext^i(F,G)⊗L∨.                                                                                                                                    | `FC06-C03-U065`, `FC06-C03-U073`, `FC06-C02-U188`                                   |
| `FC06-C03-U075` | **Proposition 6.8 — stalks of sheaf Ext**                                                             | §6 Ext Groups and Sheaves; pp. 233–238; L5845                        | If X is noetherian, F coherent, G arbitrary, and x∈X, then (sheaf Ext_X^i(F,G))*x≅Ext^i*{O_{X,x}}(F_x,G_x).                                                                                                                                                              | `FC06-C03-U067`, `FC06-C03-U070`, `FC06-C02-U052`, `FC06-C02-U111`                  |
| `FC06-C03-U076` | **Proposition 6.9 — global Ext from sheaf Ext after twisting**                                        | §6 Ext Groups and Sheaves; pp. 233–238; L5857                        | For projective X over noetherian A with very ample O(1), coherent F,G, and fixed i, Ext^i(F,G(n))≅Γ(X,sheaf Ext^i(F,G(n))) for all n≫0.                                                                                                                                  | `FC06-C03-U074`, `FC06-C03-U062`, `FC06-C03-U075`                                   |
| `FC06-C03-U077` | **Remark 6.9.1 — global-to-local Ext spectral sequence**                                              | §6 Ext Groups and Sheaves; pp. 233–238; L5887                        | On a general ringed space, global Ext and sheaf Ext are related by a spectral sequence.                                                                                                                                                                                  | `FC06-C03-U076`                                                                     |
| `FC06-C03-U078` | **Definition — projective resolution and projective dimension**                                       | §6 Ext Groups and Sheaves; pp. 233–238; L5889–5895                   | A projective resolution of an A-module M is an exact complex of projective modules ending in M; pd(M) is the least possible finite length, or +∞ if none exists.                                                                                                         | `FC06-C03-U009`                                                                     |
| `FC06-C03-U079` | **Proposition 6.10A — Ext detects projectivity and projective dimension**                             | §6 Ext Groups and Sheaves; pp. 233–238; L5897                        | M is projective iff Ext^1_A(M,N)=0 for all N; pd(M)≤n iff Ext^i_A(M,N)=0 for every i>n and every N.                                                                                                                                                                      | `FC06-C03-U078`, `FC06-C03-U065`                                                    |
| `FC06-C03-U080` | **Proposition 6.11A — projective dimension over a regular local ring**                                | §6 Ext Groups and Sheaves; pp. 233–238; L5903                        | If A is regular local, then pd(M)≤dim A for every A-module M, and for its residue field k one has pd(k)=dim A.                                                                                                                                                           | `FC06-C03-U078`, `FC06-C01-U094`                                                    |
| `FC06-C03-U081` | **Proposition 6.12A — Auslander–Buchsbaum formula**                                                   | §6 Ext Groups and Sheaves; pp. 233–238; L5909                        | If A is a regular local ring of dimension n and M is finitely generated, then pd_A(M)+depth_A(M)=n.                                                                                                                                                                      | `FC06-C03-U080`, `FC06-C03-U078`                                                    |
| `FC06-C03-U082` | **Theorem 7.1 — duality on projective space**                                                         | §7 The Serre Duality Theorem; pp. 239–249; L6011                     | For X=P^n_k with canonical sheaf ω_X: H^n(X,ω_X)≅k; Hom(F,ω_X) pairs perfectly with H^n(X,F); and Ext^i(F,ω_X)≅H^{n−i}(X,F)^∨ naturally for coherent F.                                                                                                                  | `FC06-C03-U061`, `FC06-C03-U065`, `FC06-C02-U286`                                   |
| `FC06-C03-U083` | **Remark 7.1.1 — intrinsic normalization of projective-space duality**                                | §7 The Serre Duality Theorem; pp. 239–249; L6035                     | Writing the duality theorem with the canonical sheaf makes the trace generator natural under changes of homogeneous coordinates; Hartshorne exhibits an invariant Čech cocycle generator.                                                                                | `FC06-C03-U082`, `FC06-C03-U051`                                                    |
| `FC06-C03-U084` | **Definition — dualizing sheaf and trace**                                                            | §7 The Serre Duality Theorem; pp. 239–249; L6045                     | For proper n-dimensional X/k, a dualizing sheaf is coherent ω_X^∘ with trace t:H^n(X,ω_X^∘)→k such that Hom(F,ω_X^∘)→H^n(X,F)^∨ is an isomorphism for every coherent F.                                                                                                  | `FC06-C03-U026`, `FC06-C02-U087`, `FC06-C02-U111`                                   |
| `FC06-C03-U085` | **Proposition 7.2 — uniqueness of a dualizing sheaf**                                                 | §7 The Serre Duality Theorem; pp. 239–249; L6057                     | A dualizing sheaf with trace, if it exists, is unique up to a unique isomorphism compatible with the trace.                                                                                                                                                              | `FC06-C03-U084`                                                                     |
| `FC06-C03-U086` | **Lemma 7.3 — low Ext vanishing for a projective closed subscheme**                                   | §7 The Serre Duality Theorem; pp. 239–249; L6063                     | If X⊂P^N_k is closed of codimension r, then sheaf Ext_P^i(O_X,ω_P)=0 for every i<r.                                                                                                                                                                                      | `FC06-C03-U076`, `FC06-C03-U082`, `FC06-C02-U065`                                   |
| `FC06-C03-U087` | **Lemma 7.4 — Ext representation of the candidate dualizing sheaf**                                   | §7 The Serre Duality Theorem; pp. 239–249; L6073                     | With X⊂P^N as above and ω_X^∘=sheaf Ext_P^r(O_X,ω_P), there is a functorial isomorphism Hom_X(F,ω_X^∘)≅Ext_P^r(F,ω_P) for every O_X-module F.                                                                                                                            | `FC06-C03-U086`, `FC06-C03-U065`                                                    |
| `FC06-C03-U088` | **Proposition 7.5 — projective schemes have dualizing sheaves**                                       | §7 The Serre Duality Theorem; pp. 239–249; L6093                     | Every projective scheme over a field has a dualizing sheaf, obtained from a projective embedding X⊂P^N as sheaf Ext_P^r(O_X,ω_P) with the trace induced by projective-space duality.                                                                                     | `FC06-C03-U084`, `FC06-C03-U087`, `FC06-C03-U082`                                   |
| `FC06-C03-U089` | **Theorem 7.6 — Serre duality for projective schemes**                                                | §7 The Serre Duality Theorem; pp. 239–249; L6117                     | For projective n-dimensional X over algebraically closed k with dualizing ω and very ample O(1), there are natural θ^i:Ext^i(F,ω)→H^{n−i}(F)^∨; X is Cohen–Macaulay equidimensional iff the equivalent negative-twist vanishing holds, iff every θ^i is an isomorphism.  | `FC06-C03-U088`, `FC06-C03-U065`, `FC06-C03-U062`, `FC06-C03-U081`                  |
| `FC06-C03-U090` | **Remark 7.6.1 — duality for nonsingular and lci schemes**                                            | §7 The Serre Duality Theorem; pp. 239–249; L6163                     | Nonsingular schemes and local complete intersections are Cohen–Macaulay, so the duality maps θ^i of Theorem 7.6 are isomorphisms in these cases.                                                                                                                         | `FC06-C03-U089`, `FC06-C02-U294`                                                    |
| `FC06-C03-U091` | **Corollary 7.7 — duality for locally free sheaves**                                                  | §7 The Serre Duality Theorem; pp. 239–249; L6165                     | On a projective Cohen–Macaulay equidimensional n-scheme, every locally free F satisfies H^i(X,F)≅H^{n−i}(X,F∨⊗ω_X)^∨ naturally.                                                                                                                                          | `FC06-C03-U089`, `FC06-C03-U074`                                                    |
| `FC06-C03-U092` | **Corollary 7.8 — Enriques–Severi–Zariski lemma**                                                     | §7 The Serre Duality Theorem; pp. 239–249; L6173                     | If X is normal projective of dimension at least two, then for every locally free F, H^1(X,F(−q))=0 for q≫0.                                                                                                                                                              | `FC06-C03-U089`, `FC06-C02-U290`                                                    |
| `FC06-C03-U093` | **Corollary 7.9 — connectedness of ample divisors**                                                   | §7 The Serre Duality Theorem; pp. 239–249; L6185                     | If X is integral normal projective of dimension at least two over algebraically closed k, the support of an effective ample divisor is connected.                                                                                                                        | `FC06-C03-U092`, `FC06-C02-U198`, `FC06-C02-U207`                                   |
| `FC06-C03-U094` | **Remark 7.9.1 — Bertini sections are irreducible in dimension at least two**                         | §7 The Serre Duality Theorem; pp. 239–249; L6201                     | Combining connectedness with regularity shows the hyperplane sections occurring in Bertini are irreducible as well as nonsingular when dim X≥2.                                                                                                                          | `FC06-C03-U093`, `FC06-C02-U278`                                                    |
| `FC06-C03-U095` | **Construction — Koszul complex**                                                                     | §7 The Serre Duality Theorem; pp. 239–249; L6205–6211                | For f_1,…,f_r∈A, K_•(f_1,…,f_r) is the exterior algebra on a free rank-r module with the standard contraction differential; for M set K_•(f;M)=K_•(f)⊗_A M.                                                                                                              | `FC06-C03-U009`                                                                     |
| `FC06-C03-U096` | **Proposition 7.10A — Koszul homology of a regular sequence**                                         | §7 The Serre Duality Theorem; pp. 239–249; L6213                     | If f_1,…,f_r is an M-regular sequence, then H_i(K_•(f;M))=0 for i>0 and H_0≅M/(f_1,…,f_r)M.                                                                                                                                                                              | `FC06-C03-U095`                                                                     |
| `FC06-C03-U097` | **Theorem 7.11 — dualizing sheaf of a local complete intersection**                                   | §7 The Serre Duality Theorem; pp. 239–249; L6223                     | If X⊂P^N_k is an lci of codimension r with ideal I, then ω_X^∘≅ω_P\|_X⊗(∧^r(I/I²))^∨; in particular ω_X^∘ is invertible.                                                                                                                                                 | `FC06-C03-U087`, `FC06-C03-U096`, `FC06-C02-U291`, `FC06-C02-U284`                  |
| `FC06-C03-U098` | **Corollary 7.12 — dualizing equals canonical on nonsingular projective varieties**                   | §7 The Serre Duality Theorem; pp. 239–249; L6247                     | For a projective nonsingular variety, the dualizing sheaf is isomorphic to the canonical sheaf.                                                                                                                                                                          | `FC06-C03-U097`, `FC06-C02-U285`                                                    |
| `FC06-C03-U099` | **Remark 7.12.1 — Serre duality with canonical sheaf**                                                | §7 The Serre Duality Theorem; pp. 239–249; L6251                     | For projective nonsingular X, Theorem 7.6 and Corollary 7.7 hold with the canonical sheaf; in particular H^n(X,ω_X)≅k.                                                                                                                                                   | `FC06-C03-U098`, `FC06-C03-U089`                                                    |
| `FC06-C03-U100` | **Remark 7.12.2 — arithmetic equals geometric genus for smooth projective curves**                    | §7 The Serre Duality Theorem; pp. 239–249; L6253                     | For a projective nonsingular curve, H^1(O_X) and H^0(ω_X) are dual, hence p_a=p_g.                                                                                                                                                                                       | `FC06-C03-U099`                                                                     |
| `FC06-C03-U101` | **Remark 7.12.3 — geometric genus and irregularity of a smooth projective surface**                   | §7 The Serre Duality Theorem; pp. 239–249; L6255                     | For a projective nonsingular surface, p_g=h^2(O_X), p_g≥p_a, and q=p_g−p_a=h^1(O_X) is the irregularity.                                                                                                                                                                 | `FC06-C03-U099`                                                                     |
| `FC06-C03-U102` | **Corollary 7.13 — duality for differential forms**                                                   | §7 The Serre Duality Theorem; pp. 239–249; L6257                     | If X is a nonsingular projective n-fold and Ω^p=∧^pΩ_{X/k}, then H^q(X,Ω^p)≅H^{n−q}(X,Ω^{n−p})^∨ naturally.                                                                                                                                                              | `FC06-C03-U091`, `FC06-C03-U098`, `FC06-C02-U280`                                   |
| `FC06-C03-U103` | **Remark 7.13.1 — Hodge numbers as biregular invariants**                                             | §7 The Serre Duality Theorem; pp. 239–249; L6265                     | The numbers h^{p,q}=dim H^q(X,Ω^p) are biregular invariants of a nonsingular projective variety.                                                                                                                                                                         | `FC06-C03-U102`                                                                     |
| `FC06-C03-U104` | **Remark 7.14 — residues and curve duality**                                                          | §7 The Serre Duality Theorem; pp. 239–249; L6267                     | For complete nonsingular curves, residues provide an explicit construction of the trace map and an alternative proof of duality.                                                                                                                                         | `FC06-C03-U099`                                                                     |
| `FC06-C03-U105` | **Theorem 7.14.1 — existence of residues**                                                            | §7 The Serre Duality Theorem; pp. 239–249; L6271                     | For each closed P on a complete nonsingular curve with function field K there is a unique k-linear res_P:Ω_{K/k}→k vanishing on regular differentials, killing f^n df for n≠−1, and sending f^{-1}df to v_P(f).                                                          | `FC06-C03-U104`, `FC06-C02-U265`, `FC06-C01-U118`                                   |
| `FC06-C03-U106` | **Theorem 7.14.2 — residue theorem**                                                                  | §7 The Serre Duality Theorem; pp. 239–249; L6287                     | For every rational differential τ on a complete nonsingular curve, the finite sum Σ_P res_P(τ) over closed points is zero.                                                                                                                                               | `FC06-C03-U105`                                                                     |
| `FC06-C03-U107` | **Construction — duality trace from the sum of residues**                                             | §7 The Serre Duality Theorem; pp. 239–249; L6293–6323                | Using the flasque resolution of O_X by the constant function-field sheaf and its quotient, tensoring with Ω_X identifies H^1(X,Ω_X) as a quotient of ⊕_P Ω_K/Ω_P; the sum of residues descends to the Serre-duality trace H^1(X,Ω_X)→k.                                  | `FC06-C03-U106`, `FC06-C03-U030`                                                    |
| `FC06-C03-U108` | **Remark 7.15 — Kodaira vanishing theorem**                                                           | §7 The Serre Duality Theorem; pp. 239–249; L6325                     | For smooth projective X/C of dimension n and ample L, H^i(X,L⊗ω_X)=0 for i>0, equivalently by Serre duality H^i(X,L^{-1})=0 for i<n; Hartshorne notes characteristic-p counterexamples.                                                                                  | `FC06-C03-U098`, `FC06-C03-U091`, `FC06-C02-U207`                                   |
| `FC06-C03-U109` | **Definition — higher direct images**                                                                 | §8 Higher Direct Images of Sheaves; pp. 250–252; L6360               | For continuous f:X→Y, R^i f_*:Ab(X)→Ab(Y) is the ith right derived functor of the left exact direct-image functor f_*.                                                                                                                                                   | `FC06-C03-U013`, `FC06-C02-U019`, `FC06-C03-U025`                                   |
| `FC06-C03-U110` | **Proposition 8.1 — local cohomology description of R^i f_***                                         | §8 Higher Direct Images of Sheaves; pp. 250–252; L6364               | R^i f_*F is the sheaf associated to the presheaf V↦H^i(f^{-1}V,F\|_{f^{-1}V}).                                                                                                                                                                                           | `FC06-C03-U109`, `FC06-C03-U026`, `FC06-C02-U012`                                   |
| `FC06-C03-U111` | **Corollary 8.2 — restriction of higher direct images**                                               | §8 Higher Direct Images of Sheaves; pp. 250–252; L6376               | For open V⊆Y and f′:f^{-1}V→V, (R^i f_*F)\|*V≅R^i f′**(F\|_{f^{-1}V}).                                                                                                                                                                                                   | `FC06-C03-U110`                                                                     |
| `FC06-C03-U112` | **Corollary 8.3 — flasque sheaves are f_*-acyclic**                                                   | §8 Higher Direct Images of Sheaves; pp. 250–252; L6386               | If F is flasque on X, then R^i f_*F=0 for every i>0.                                                                                                                                                                                                                     | `FC06-C03-U110`, `FC06-C03-U029`                                                    |
| `FC06-C03-U113` | **Proposition 8.4 — higher direct images in module categories**                                       | §8 Higher Direct Images of Sheaves; pp. 250–252; L6390               | For a morphism of ringed spaces, R^i f_* may be computed as the derived functors of f_*:Mod(X)→Mod(Y).                                                                                                                                                                   | `FC06-C03-U109`, `FC06-C03-U028`, `FC06-C03-U016`                                   |
| `FC06-C03-U114` | **Proposition 8.5 — affine-target description**                                                       | §8 Higher Direct Images of Sheaves; pp. 250–252; L6394               | If X is noetherian, Y=Spec A, f:X→Y, and F quasi-coherent, then R^i f_*F≅~H^i(X,F).                                                                                                                                                                                      | `FC06-C03-U113`, `FC06-C03-U046`, `FC06-C02-U119`                                   |
| `FC06-C03-U115` | **Corollary 8.6 — higher direct images preserve quasi-coherence**                                     | §8 Higher Direct Images of Sheaves; pp. 250–252; L6406               | If X is noetherian and f:X→Y is a scheme morphism, then R^i f_*F is quasi-coherent for every quasi-coherent F.                                                                                                                                                           | `FC06-C03-U114`                                                                     |
| `FC06-C03-U116` | **Proposition 8.7 — Čech computation of higher direct images**                                        | §8 Higher Direct Images of Sheaves; pp. 250–252; L6410               | For f between separated noetherian schemes, quasi-coherent F, and an affine open cover U of X, R^p f_*F≅h^p(f_*C^•(U,F)).                                                                                                                                                | `FC06-C03-U115`, `FC06-C03-U059`, `FC06-C02-U078`                                   |
| `FC06-C03-U117` | **Theorem 8.8 — projective direct-image finiteness and vanishing**                                    | §8 Higher Direct Images of Sheaves; pp. 250–252; L6424               | For projective f:X→Y of noetherian schemes, very ample O_X(1), and coherent F: f^*f_*F(n)→F(n) is surjective for n≫0; every R^i f_*F is coherent; and R^i f_*F(n)=0 for i>0,n≫0.                                                                                         | `FC06-C03-U114`, `FC06-C03-U062`, `FC06-C02-U091`, `FC06-C02-U140`                  |
| `FC06-C03-U118` | **Remark 8.8.1 — proper direct-image coherence**                                                      | §8 Higher Direct Images of Sheaves; pp. 250–252; L6431               | The coherence assertion for R^i f_* remains true for proper morphisms of noetherian schemes; Hartshorne notes Grauert’s analytic analogue.                                                                                                                               | `FC06-C03-U117`, `FC06-C02-U087`                                                    |
| `FC06-C03-U119` | **Definition — flat module and flat algebra**                                                         | §9 Flat Morphisms; pp. 253–267; L6476                                | An A-module M is flat when N↦M⊗_A N is exact; a ring map A→B is flat when B is flat as an A-module.                                                                                                                                                                      | `FC06-C03-U010`                                                                     |
| `FC06-C03-U120` | **Proposition 9.1A — criteria and stability for flat modules**                                        | §9 Flat Morphisms; pp. 253–267; L6478                                | Flatness is equivalent to injectivity a⊗M→M for every finitely generated ideal a; it is preserved by base extension and transitivity, is local at primes, behaves in short exact sequences as stated, and over a local noetherian ring a finite module is flat iff free. | `FC06-C03-U119`, `FC06-C02-U052`                                                    |
| `FC06-C03-U121` | **Example 9.1.1 — localization is flat**                                                              | §9 Flat Morphisms; pp. 253–267; L6488                                | Every localization S^{-1}A is flat over A; if a B-module M is A-flat, then S^{-1}M is also A-flat.                                                                                                                                                                       | `FC06-C03-U120`                                                                     |
| `FC06-C03-U122` | **Example 9.1.2 — noetherian adic completion is flat**                                                | §9 Flat Morphisms; pp. 253–267; L6490                                | For noetherian A and ideal a, the a-adic completion Â is a flat A-algebra.                                                                                                                                                                                               | `FC06-C03-U120`, `FC06-C02-U309`                                                    |
| `FC06-C03-U123` | **Example 9.1.3 — flat modules over a PID are torsion-free modules**                                  | §9 Flat Morphisms; pp. 253–267; L6492                                | For a PID A, an A-module is flat iff torsion-free.                                                                                                                                                                                                                       | `FC06-C03-U120`                                                                     |
| `FC06-C03-U124` | **Definition — sheaf flat over a base and flat morphism**                                             | §9 Flat Morphisms; pp. 253–267; L6494                                | For f:X→Y and O_X-module F, F is flat over Y at x when F_x is flat over O_{Y,f(x)}; it is flat over Y if so everywhere. X is flat over Y when O_X is.                                                                                                                    | `FC06-C03-U119`, `FC06-C02-U008`                                                    |
| `FC06-C03-U125` | **Proposition 9.2 — stability and affine criteria for scheme flatness**                               | §9 Flat Morphisms; pp. 253–267; L6496                                | Open immersions are flat; flatness of sheaves is preserved by base change and transitivity; for Spec B→Spec A, M~ is flat over Spec A iff M is A-flat; on noetherian X, coherent F is flat over X iff locally free.                                                      | `FC06-C03-U124`, `FC06-C03-U120`, `FC06-C02-U074`, `FC06-C02-U110`, `FC06-C02-U103` |
| `FC06-C03-U126` | **Proposition 9.3 — cohomology commutes with flat base extension**                                    | §9 Flat Morphisms; pp. 253–267; L6504                                | In a cartesian square obtained from a separated finite-type morphism f:X→Y of noetherian schemes by flat u:Y′→Y, for quasi-coherent F there are natural isomorphisms u^*R^i f_*F≅R^i g_*(v^*F) for all i.                                                                | `FC06-C03-U125`, `FC06-C03-U116`, `FC06-C02-U074`                                   |
| `FC06-C03-U127` | **Remark 9.3.1 — natural base-change map without flatness**                                           | §9 Flat Morphisms; pp. 253–267; L6524                                | For arbitrary base change there is still a natural comparison u^*R^i f_*F→R^i g_*(v^*F), though it need not be an isomorphism.                                                                                                                                           | `FC06-C03-U126`                                                                     |
| `FC06-C03-U128` | **Corollary 9.4 — cohomology of a fibre as tensor with its residue field**                            | §9 Flat Morphisms; pp. 253–267; L6526                                | Under Proposition 9.3 with Y affine, for y∈Y and induced F_y on X_y, H^i(X_y,F_y)≅H^i(X,F⊗k(y)) naturally for all i.                                                                                                                                                     | `FC06-C03-U126`, `FC06-C02-U071`                                                    |
| `FC06-C03-U129` | **Definition/convention — flat family of schemes**                                                    | §9 Flat Morphisms; pp. 253–267; L6544                                | A flat family is a family of fibres of a flat morphism; Hartshorne adopts flatness as the scheme-theoretic condition expressing a continuously varying algebraic family.                                                                                                 | `FC06-C03-U124`, `FC06-C02-U071`                                                    |
| `FC06-C03-U130` | **Proposition 9.5 — dimension formula for flat morphisms**                                            | §9 Flat Morphisms; pp. 253–267; L6546                                | For flat f:X→Y of schemes finite type over a field and x∈X with y=f(x), dim_x X_y=dim_x X−dim_y Y.                                                                                                                                                                       | `FC06-C03-U124`, `FC06-C02-U065`                                                    |
| `FC06-C03-U131` | **Corollary 9.6 — equidimensional total space versus fibres**                                         | §9 Flat Morphisms; pp. 253–267; L6560                                | If f:X→Y is flat finite type over a field and Y irreducible, then all irreducible components of X have dimension dim Y+n iff every irreducible component of every fibre has dimension n.                                                                                 | `FC06-C03-U130`, `FC06-C02-U047`                                                    |
| `FC06-C03-U132` | **Definition — associated point of a scheme**                                                         | §9 Flat Morphisms; pp. 253–267; L6600                                | A point x∈X is associated if the maximal ideal of O_{X,x} is an associated prime of zero, equivalently every element of that maximal ideal is a zero divisor.                                                                                                            | `FC06-C02-U008`                                                                     |
| `FC06-C03-U133` | **Proposition 9.7 — flatness over a regular integral curve**                                          | §9 Flat Morphisms; pp. 253–267; L6602                                | If Y is regular integral of dimension one, f:X→Y is flat iff every associated point of X maps to the generic point of Y; for reduced X this means every irreducible component dominates Y.                                                                               | `FC06-C03-U132`, `FC06-C03-U123`, `FC06-C03-U124`, `FC06-C02-U151`                  |
| `FC06-C03-U134` | **Example 9.7.1 — normalization of a nodal curve is not flat**                                        | §9 Flat Morphisms; pp. 253–267; L6610                                | The normalization map of a nodal curve is not flat: its finite pushforward has rank one generically but needs two generators at the node, hence is not locally free there.                                                                                               | `FC06-C03-U133`, `FC06-C03-U125`                                                    |
| `FC06-C03-U135` | **Example 9.7.2 — dominance over a regular higher-dimensional base does not imply flatness**          | §9 Flat Morphisms; pp. 253–267; L6612                                | Blowing up a point of A² gives a dominant map between nonsingular schemes that is not flat because the exceptional fibre has dimension too large.                                                                                                                        | `FC06-C03-U130`, `FC06-C02-U248`                                                    |
| `FC06-C03-U136` | **Proposition 9.8 — unique flat limit over a missing point of a regular curve**                       | §9 Flat Morphisms; pp. 253–267; L6614                                | If Y is regular integral one-dimensional, P∈Y closed, and X⊂P^n_{Y−P} is closed and flat over Y−P, then its scheme-theoretic closure in P^n_Y is the unique closed subscheme extending X and flat over Y.                                                                | `FC06-C03-U133`, `FC06-C02-U091`                                                    |
| `FC06-C03-U137` | **Remark 9.8.1 — valuative properness of the Hilbert scheme**                                         | §9 Flat Morphisms; pp. 253–267; L6618                                | Proposition 9.8 is the essential valuative “pass to the limit” statement showing connected components of the Hilbert scheme of subschemes of projective space are proper.                                                                                                | `FC06-C03-U136`, `FC06-C02-U089`                                                    |
| `FC06-C03-U138` | **Example 9.8.2 — flat families can acquire reducible or nonreduced special fibres**                  | §9 Flat Morphisms; pp. 253–267; L6622                                | The earlier degenerations ay=x² and xy=t are flat families whose general fibres are integral but whose special fibres are respectively nonreduced and reducible.                                                                                                         | `FC06-C03-U133`, `FC06-C02-U076`, `FC06-C02-U077`                                   |
| `FC06-C03-U139` | **Example 9.8.3 — projection as a flat limit**                                                        | §9 Flat Morphisms; pp. 253–267; L6624                                | Scaling one coordinate of a projective subscheme produces a trivial flat family over A¹{0}; Proposition 9.8 extends it uniquely over 0, where the support is the projection from the scaling center.                                                                     | `FC06-C03-U136`                                                                     |
| `FC06-C03-U140` | **Example 9.8.4 — twisted cubic degenerates to a nodal cubic with an embedded point**                 | §9 Flat Morphisms; pp. 253–267; L6628–6661                           | For a projected twisted cubic, the unique flat limit has support the nodal plane cubic but carries a nonzero square-zero embedded element at its node; the nilpotent records the lost transverse direction.                                                              | `FC06-C03-U139`, `FC06-C03-U129`                                                    |
| `FC06-C03-U141` | **Example 9.8.5 — algebraic families of Cartier divisors and flatness**                               | §9 Flat Morphisms; pp. 253–267; L6663                                | For effective Cartier D on X×T with T a nonsingular curve, fibrewise intersections D_t are defined when local equations remain nonzerodivisors; these form an algebraic family of divisors, and D→T is flat iff every D_t is defined.                                    | `FC06-C03-U133`, `FC06-C02-U198`                                                    |
| `FC06-C03-U142` | **Theorem 9.9 — flatness criterion by Hilbert polynomial**                                            | §9 Flat Morphisms; pp. 253–267; L6667                                | For integral noetherian T and closed X⊂P^n_T, X is flat over T iff the Hilbert polynomial of the fibre X_t is independent of t.                                                                                                                                          | `FC06-C03-U129`, `FC06-C03-U062`, `FC06-C02-U044`                                   |
| `FC06-C03-U143` | **Corollary 9.10 — dimension, degree, and arithmetic genus are constant in projective flat families** | §9 Flat Morphisms; pp. 253–267; L6747                                | For connected noetherian T and closed X⊂P^n_T flat over T, fibre dimension, degree, and arithmetic genus are independent of t.                                                                                                                                           | `FC06-C03-U142`, `FC06-C01-U141`                                                    |
| `FC06-C03-U144` | **Definition — algebraic family of varieties**                                                        | §9 Flat Morphisms; pp. 253–267; L6761–6765                           | For surjective f:X→T of varieties over algebraically closed k, the reduced fibres X_(t) form an algebraic family when each is irreducible of expected dimension and the scheme-theoretic fibre is reduced at its generic point (multiplicity one).                       | `FC06-C03-U129`, `FC06-C02-U064`                                                    |
| `FC06-C03-U145` | **Example 9.10.1 — reduced fibres can lose flat invariants**                                          | §9 Flat Morphisms; pp. 253–267; L6767                                | Taking reduced fibres in Example 9.8.4 gives an algebraic family of varieties whose arithmetic genus jumps from 0 to 1; the nilpotent embedded point in the flat scheme fibre is exactly what restores constancy.                                                        | `FC06-C03-U140`, `FC06-C03-U144`, `FC06-C03-U143`                                   |
| `FC06-C03-U146` | **Theorem 9.11 — algebraic families of normal varieties over a smooth curve are flat**                | §9 Flat Morphisms; pp. 253–267; L6769                                | An algebraic family of normal varieties parametrized by a nonsingular curve over algebraically closed k is a flat family of schemes.                                                                                                                                     | `FC06-C03-U144`, `FC06-C03-U133`                                                    |
| `FC06-C03-U147` | **Lemma 9.12 — Hironaka lemma**                                                                       | §9 Flat Morphisms; pp. 253–267; L6773                                | Let A be a local noetherian finite-type domain over a field and t∈A. If tA has one minimal associated prime p, t generates the maximal ideal of A_p, and A/p is normal, then p=tA and A is normal.                                                                       | `FC06-C03-U132`, `FC06-C02-U290`                                                    |
| `FC06-C03-U148` | **Corollary 9.13 — Igusa constancy theorem**                                                          | §9 Flat Morphisms; pp. 253–267; L6795                                | For an algebraic family of normal projective varieties parametrized by a variety T, the Hilbert polynomial, hence arithmetic genus, is independent of t.                                                                                                                 | `FC06-C03-U146`, `FC06-C03-U142`                                                    |
| `FC06-C03-U149` | **Example 9.13.1 — infinitesimal deformation over dual numbers**                                      | §9 Flat Morphisms; pp. 253–267; L6799–6801                           | For D=k[t]/(t²), an infinitesimal deformation of finite-type X_0/k is a D-flat scheme X′ with X′⊗_D k≅X_0; tangent vectors to a base of a global flat family produce such deformations by base change.                                                                   | `FC06-C03-U125`, `FC06-C02-U074`                                                    |
| `FC06-C03-U150` | **Example 9.13.2 — infinitesimal deformations of a nonsingular scheme are classified by H¹(T_X)**     | §9 Flat Morphisms; pp. 253–267; L6803–6817                           | For nonsingular X over algebraically closed k, infinitesimal deformations up to isomorphism correspond to H^1(X,T_X); flatness identifies a deformation with an infinitesimal extension by O_X.                                                                          | `FC06-C03-U149`, `FC06-C03-U026`, `FC06-C02-U280`                                   |
| `FC06-C03-U151` | **Remark 9.13.3 — deformation-theory tangent and obstruction spaces**                                 | §9 Flat Morphisms; pp. 253–267; L6819–6823                           | Hartshorne records the deformation-theoretic principle that first-order deformations are controlled by H^1(T_X), automorphisms by H^0(T_X), and obstructions live in H^2(T_X) in the nonsingular case.                                                                   | `FC06-C03-U150`                                                                     |
| `FC06-C03-U152` | **Definition — smooth morphism of relative dimension n**                                              | §10 Smooth Morphisms; pp. 268–275; L6904–6912                        | For finite-type k-schemes, f:X→Y is smooth of relative dimension n when it is flat, corresponding irreducible components differ in dimension by n, and dim_{k(x)}(Ω_{X/Y}⊗k(x))=n at every x.                                                                            | `FC06-C03-U124`, `FC06-C02-U265`, `FC06-C02-U065`                                   |
| `FC06-C03-U153` | **Example 10.0.1 — relative affine and projective spaces are smooth**                                 | §10 Smooth Morphisms; pp. 268–275; L6914                             | A^n_Y and P^n_Y are smooth of relative dimension n over every finite-type k-scheme Y.                                                                                                                                                                                    | `FC06-C03-U152`, `FC06-C02-U044`                                                    |
| `FC06-C03-U154` | **Example 10.0.2 — differential criterion on an integral source**                                     | §10 Smooth Morphisms; pp. 268–275; L6916                             | When X is integral, the differential-rank condition in smoothness is equivalent to Ω_{X/Y} being locally free of rank n.                                                                                                                                                 | `FC06-C03-U152`, `FC06-C02-U264`                                                    |
| `FC06-C03-U155` | **Example 10.0.3 — smooth over an algebraically closed field equals regular**                         | §10 Smooth Morphisms; pp. 268–275; L6918                             | Over algebraically closed k, X is smooth of dimension n over Spec k iff X is regular of dimension n; for irreducible separated X this is equivalent to being a nonsingular variety.                                                                                      | `FC06-C03-U152`, `FC06-C02-U273`                                                    |
| `FC06-C03-U156` | **Proposition 10.1 — stability of smooth morphisms**                                                  | §10 Smooth Morphisms; pp. 268–275; L6920                             | Open immersions are smooth of relative dimension 0; smoothness and relative dimension are preserved by base change; relative dimensions add under composition and fibre products.                                                                                        | `FC06-C03-U152`, `FC06-C03-U125`, `FC06-C02-U268`, `FC06-C02-U269`                  |
| `FC06-C03-U157` | **Theorem 10.2 — smoothness via geometrically regular fibres**                                        | §10 Smooth Morphisms; pp. 268–275; L6974                             | A finite-type morphism f is smooth of relative dimension n iff f is flat and every geometric fibre is regular and equidimensional of dimension n.                                                                                                                        | `FC06-C03-U156`, `FC06-C03-U131`, `FC06-C02-U263`                                   |
| `FC06-C03-U158` | **Lemma 10.3A — one-element local criterion for flatness**                                            | §10 Smooth Morphisms; pp. 268–275; L6996                             | For a local map A→B of noetherian local rings, finite B-module M, and nonunit nonzerodivisor t∈A, M is A-flat iff t is M-regular and M/tM is flat over A/tA.                                                                                                             | `FC06-C03-U120`                                                                     |
| `FC06-C03-U159` | **Proposition 10.4 — tangent-space criterion for smoothness between nonsingular varieties**           | §10 Smooth Morphisms; pp. 268–275; L7002                             | For f:X→Y between nonsingular varieties over algebraically closed k and n=dim X−dim Y, the following are equivalent: f smooth of relative dimension n; Ω_{X/Y} locally free rank n; every closed-point tangent map T_xX→T_{f(x)}Y is surjective.                         | `FC06-C03-U152`, `FC06-C03-U158`, `FC06-C02-U269`, `FC06-C02-U262`                  |
| `FC06-C03-U160` | **Lemma 10.5 — generic smoothness on the source in characteristic zero**                              | §10 Smooth Morphisms; pp. 268–275; L7044                             | A dominant morphism of integral finite-type schemes over algebraically closed characteristic-zero k is smooth on some nonempty open subset of the source.                                                                                                                | `FC06-C03-U159`, `FC06-C02-U276`, `FC06-C01-U081`                                   |
| `FC06-C03-U161` | **Example 10.5.1 — Frobenius is nowhere smooth**                                                      | §10 Smooth Morphisms; pp. 268–275; L7048                             | In characteristic p, Frobenius P^1→P^1 has relative dimension 0 but Ω_{X/Y} has rank 1 because d(t^p)=0, so it is nowhere smooth.                                                                                                                                        | `FC06-C03-U154`, `FC06-C02-U265`                                                    |
| `FC06-C03-U162` | **Proposition 10.6 — dimension bound for images of low-rank tangent loci**                            | §10 Smooth Morphisms; pp. 268–275; L7050                             | In characteristic zero, for f:X→Y finite type and X_r the closed points where rank T_{f,x}≤r, one has dim closure(f(X_r))≤r.                                                                                                                                             | `FC06-C03-U160`, `FC06-C03-U159`                                                    |
| `FC06-C03-U163` | **Corollary 10.7 — generic smoothness**                                                               | §10 Smooth Morphisms; pp. 268–275; L7070                             | If f:X→Y is a morphism of varieties over algebraically closed characteristic-zero k with X nonsingular, there is a nonempty open V⊆Y such that f:f^{-1}(V)→V is smooth.                                                                                                  | `FC06-C03-U162`, `FC06-C03-U159`                                                    |
| `FC06-C03-U164` | **Definitions — group variety action and homogeneous space**                                          | §10 Smooth Morphisms; pp. 268–275; L7076–7080                        | A group variety is a variety with multiplication and inversion inducing a group on k-points; an action is a morphism G×X→X inducing a group homomorphism G(k)→Aut X; a homogeneous space has a transitive G(k)-action.                                                   | `FC06-C02-U095`                                                                     |
| `FC06-C03-U165` | **Remark 10.7.1 — a group variety acts transitively on itself**                                       | §10 Smooth Morphisms; pp. 268–275; L7082                             | Every group variety is a homogeneous space under left multiplication.                                                                                                                                                                                                    | `FC06-C03-U164`                                                                     |
| `FC06-C03-U166` | **Example 10.7.2 — projective space is homogeneous under PGL**                                        | §10 Smooth Morphisms; pp. 268–275; L7084                             | P^n_k is a homogeneous space under the standard PGL action.                                                                                                                                                                                                              | `FC06-C03-U164`, `FC06-C02-U202`                                                    |
| `FC06-C03-U167` | **Example 10.7.3 — homogeneous spaces are nonsingular**                                               | §10 Smooth Morphisms; pp. 268–275; L7086                             | Every homogeneous space over algebraically closed k is nonsingular because a nonempty nonsingular open exists and transitivity transports it to every closed point.                                                                                                      | `FC06-C03-U164`, `FC06-C02-U276`                                                    |
| `FC06-C03-U168` | **Theorem 10.8 — Kleiman transversality**                                                             | §10 Smooth Morphisms; pp. 268–275; L7088–7136                        | Let homogeneous X with group G be over algebraically closed characteristic-zero k, and let nonsingular Y,Z map to X. For σ in a nonempty open of G, Y^σ×_X Z is nonsingular and, if nonempty, has dimension dim Y+dim Z−dim X.                                           | `FC06-C03-U163`, `FC06-C03-U156`, `FC06-C03-U167`, `FC06-C02-U070`                  |
| `FC06-C03-U169` | **Corollary 10.9 — Bertini for base-point-free systems**                                              | §10 Smooth Morphisms; pp. 268–275; L7138                             | On a nonsingular projective variety over algebraically closed characteristic-zero k, a general member of a base-point-free linear system is nonsingular, though it may be reducible.                                                                                     | `FC06-C03-U168`, `FC06-C02-U223`, `FC06-C02-U222`                                   |
| `FC06-C03-U170` | **Remark 10.9.1 — connectedness makes general members irreducible**                                   | §10 Smooth Morphisms; pp. 268–275; L7142                             | If the image of the linear-system map has dimension at least two, later connectedness implies general members are irreducible as well as nonsingular.                                                                                                                    | `FC06-C03-U169`                                                                     |
| `FC06-C03-U171` | **Remark 10.9.2 — projectivity is unnecessary for finite-dimensional systems**                        | §10 Smooth Morphisms; pp. 268–275; L7144                             | For a finite-dimensional linear system, projectivity of X is unnecessary; with base locus Σ on a projective X, a general member can have singularities only at Σ.                                                                                                        | `FC06-C03-U169`, `FC06-C02-U221`                                                    |
| `FC06-C03-U172` | **Remark 10.9.3 — Bertini can fail in characteristic p**                                              | §10 Smooth Morphisms; pp. 268–275; L7146                             | Characteristic-p Frobenius yields a linear system whose every divisor is a point with multiplicity p, showing the characteristic-zero Bertini statement can fail.                                                                                                        | `FC06-C03-U169`, `FC06-C03-U161`                                                    |
| `FC06-C03-U173` | **Remark 10.9.4 — comparison with the earlier hyperplane Bertini theorem**                            | §10 Smooth Morphisms; pp. 268–275; L7148                             | Hartshorne compares the linear-system Bertini theorem with II.8.18, the earlier hyperplane-section form.                                                                                                                                                                 | `FC06-C03-U169`, `FC06-C02-U278`                                                    |
| `FC06-C03-U174` | **Construction — infinitesimal neighbourhoods of a projective fibre**                                 | §11 The Theorem on Formal Functions; pp. 276–280; L7188–7202         | For projective f:X→Y, coherent F, y∈Y, and n≥1, set X_n=X×*Y Spec(O*{Y,y}/m_y^n) and F_n=v^*F; X_1 is the fibre and higher X_n are nilpotent thickenings with the same underlying space.                                                                                 | `FC06-C02-U071`, `FC06-C02-U074`, `FC06-C02-U106`                                   |
| `FC06-C03-U175` | **Construction — completion-to-formal-cohomology comparison map**                                     | §11 The Theorem on Formal Functions; pp. 276–280; L7202–7212         | The base-change maps R^i f_*F⊗O_y/m_y^n→H^i(X_n,F_n) form inverse systems, yielding a natural map from the completed stalk (R^i f_*F)_y^ to lim←H^i(X_n,F_n).                                                                                                            | `FC06-C03-U174`, `FC06-C03-U127`, `FC06-C03-U114`, `FC06-C02-U297`                  |
| `FC06-C03-U176` | **Theorem 11.1 — theorem on formal functions**                                                        | §11 The Theorem on Formal Functions; pp. 276–280; L7214–7220         | For projective f:X→Y of noetherian schemes, coherent F, and y∈Y, the natural map (R^i f_*F)_y^→lim←H^i(X_n,F_n) is an isomorphism for every i≥0.                                                                                                                         | `FC06-C03-U175`, `FC06-C03-U117`, `FC06-C02-U309`, `FC06-C02-U301`                  |
| `FC06-C03-U177` | **Remark 11.1.1 — formal functions for proper morphisms**                                             | §11 The Theorem on Formal Functions; pp. 276–280; L7304              | Grothendieck proves the formal-functions theorem more generally for proper morphisms.                                                                                                                                                                                    | `FC06-C03-U176`, `FC06-C02-U087`                                                    |
| `FC06-C03-U178` | **Remark 11.1.2 — degree-zero formal functions**                                                      | §11 The Theorem on Formal Functions; pp. 276–280; L7306              | For i=0 the inverse limit is Γ(X^,F^) on the formal completion along X_y; for F=O_X it is the ring of formal-regular (holomorphic) functions along the fibre.                                                                                                            | `FC06-C03-U176`, `FC06-C02-U310`, `FC06-C02-U312`                                   |
| `FC06-C03-U179` | **Remark 11.1.3 — cohomology on the formal completion**                                               | §11 The Theorem on Formal Functions; pp. 276–280; L7308              | One can define H^i(X^,F^) on the formal scheme and identify it with both quantities in the formal-functions theorem.                                                                                                                                                     | `FC06-C03-U178`                                                                     |
| `FC06-C03-U180` | **Corollary 11.2 — higher direct images vanish above fibre dimension**                                | §11 The Theorem on Formal Functions; pp. 276–280; L7310              | If f:X→Y is projective noetherian and r=max_y dim X_y, then R^i f_*F=0 for every coherent F and i>r.                                                                                                                                                                     | `FC06-C03-U176`, `FC06-C03-U033`, `FC06-C03-U117`                                   |
| `FC06-C03-U181` | **Corollary 11.3 — f_*O_X=O_Y forces connected fibres**                                               | §11 The Theorem on Formal Functions; pp. 276–280; L7320              | If projective f:X→Y of noetherian schemes satisfies f_*O_X=O_Y, then every fibre f^{-1}(y) is connected.                                                                                                                                                                 | `FC06-C03-U178`, `FC06-C02-U047`                                                    |
| `FC06-C03-U182` | **Corollary 11.4 — Zariski Main Theorem, connected-fibre form**                                       | §11 The Theorem on Formal Functions; pp. 276–280; L7344              | If f:X→Y is birational projective between noetherian integral schemes and Y is normal, then every fibre is connected.                                                                                                                                                    | `FC06-C03-U181`, `FC06-C02-U049`, `FC06-C02-U290`                                   |
| `FC06-C03-U183` | **Corollary 11.5 — Stein factorization**                                                              | §11 The Theorem on Formal Functions; pp. 276–280; L7348              | Every projective morphism f:X→Y of noetherian schemes factors X→Y′→Y with the first map projective with connected fibres and the second finite; one takes Y′=Spec_Y f_*O_X.                                                                                              | `FC06-C03-U181`, `FC06-C02-U056`, `FC06-C02-U091`                                   |
| `FC06-C03-U184` | **Definition — fibre-cohomology δ-functors T^i**                                                      | §12 The Semicontinuity Theorem; pp. 281–292; L7397–7403              | For noetherian A, projective f:X→Spec A, and coherent F flat over A, define T^i(M)=H^i(X,F⊗_A M) for every A-module M.                                                                                                                                                   | `FC06-C03-U124`, `FC06-C03-U026`, `FC06-C02-U091`                                   |
